# Public-plus FINDINGS: our findings against the proven public files (alygg77@6cae49a)

Generated from the read-only workflow `wf_1949358b-dd0` (journal in `research/public-exposed-wf_1949358b-dd0/`).
That run had 5 inventory agents, 5 adversarial verifiers and 1 "why" agent, and finished 2026-10-31 00:14Z.
PLAN.md sections 3-4 hold the summary.

**Contamination note:** this file describes the public kernels at line level. arch-mirror clean-room writers must count reading it as a public read.

Verifier caveats:
- Each problem's verifier listed misclassified inventory rows (about 11% overall). They are in the per-problem "verifier corrections" lists.
- Line refs are into `~/git/okc-muse-spark-kernels/kernels/<problem>.py` (PUB) unless a path says otherwise.

**What the controls establish:**
- **Their published rows reproduce, as slightly high draws.** Use 1.54 (bf16) and 2.37 (moe) as same-day targets.
- **Prepare time.** Their moe prepare ran in 164-167 s on the judge, not the 273-312 s their FACTS feared.
- **Headline A/A spread is about 0.5%.** An arm needs about +0.5% headline to read in 2 checks.
- **Per-row spread is large.** It reaches 6.3% (moe d16), 5.8% (moe v1) and 7.8-8.4% (bf16 router v1/v16, o v16).
  A row-level claim needs rows with s_r < 2% or more reads.
- **Today's public read is faster than our board row on every row of both problems.** The 0-of-68 finding holds
  against same-day public times. The closest are bf16 unpooler v16 (1.002) and router d1 (1.011).
- **Judge per-kernel view (first ever, public-exposed):**
  - Their moe runs ONE kernel per row: `okc::moe_fused<false>` at T<=32, `<true>` (WGS) at T=128/256. Ours runs 3-4:
    ea6/ea4x w13 + w2 + combine, plus wg at v32.
  - Their bf16 rows run, in order: cgemv (m=1); sk2 (m=8..32); Nathen's cps (pooler m16); smallm2 (pooler m8); v18s
    (o m8); rr_small (router m8/16); qb_kernel (qkv m128); ol/rl projections (o m128/256); cuBLASLt nvjet pins
    (pooler/unpooler/qkv m128/256); rk (router m128/256).

**Is the control within noise of the published row? (checked 10-31 ~01:00Z, after the user asked)**
- **Our nonce line cannot change execution.** The control bytes differ from the public file only by the comment on
  line 2. Neither file reads its own source. Their extension names hash the CUDA source strings (bf16 PUB:4081, 4318,
  4511, 4543, 4636), and those strings are unchanged. So the compiled kernels are identical.
- **bf16 is exactly at their own mean.** alygg77 submitted this same file 4 times: 1.5443 (sfo3), 1.5410 (sfo3c),
  1.5502 (sfo3c) and 1.5259 (sfo3) (H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md §3). Mean 1.5404, sd 1.0%. Our 2 reads also average
  **1.5404**. The published 1.5502 is the best of those 4, because the board keeps a team's best scored run, so a
  board row is biased high.
- **moe is consistent with noise but not proven.** Our mean score is 5673 µs against their 5600 µs (+1.3%; the same
  SGLang baseline_us of 13448.5). All 6 rows are 0.7-3.7% slower, a uniform shift. That is inside their documented
  unchanged-file board spread of 1.6-2.2%, their sfo3/sfo3c moe offset of 0.9-1.8% per load, and their 5-10 µs v32
  container spread (ours is +3.9 µs). They published no repeat runs for moe, and their 2.4015 is also a best-run board
  row. A third read (1 check) would tighten this; that needs the user's go.
- **Reading rule.** Judge arms against the same-day control, never the published row. Compare ours with theirs best
  against best, or mean against mean. Our board rows are best-of-N too.

## Why the gap persists (why-gap agent)

The "right answer" is a whole file, not a list of values or decisions you can move one at a time. The public boards are vjeux's architecture: attention and prefill are vjeux's files unchanged, and decode, moe and bf16 start from them (PUB/README.md:48-52). alygg77's own documented gains on top are small: KPERM3 +1.25% on moe, about +2.4% on bf16, nothing above noise on attention or prefill. Their FACTS and NEGATIVE files describe those small increments and what failed. They do not explain why vjeux's structure is fast, and our measured facts already agree with most of them, so knowing them adds little speed. The speed lives in co-designed structure: one templated attention kernel on all 12 rows against our 23 kernels and 2-3 launches; one persistent moe kernel against our multi-engine pipeline; a 12-bit codec inside a kernel built to absorb its decode. Their values (2 of 2 failed) and their single decisions (T1 +1.9 us, AT1 +18.6 us, S3 +5.4 us) fail on our code because they only work inside their bundle. Rebuilding a bundle clean-room from a no-code spec lost the cost-critical details: the empty moe skeleton alone cost 17-19 us, against their entire 24.6 us decode-1 row. What did work was replacing whole components with mechanisms whose cost is per-SM work or latency, which carries over to the H200: 16 landings, x1.25 for the day, with S1 (a whole-kernel bundle) the largest at +4.2%. At about 1.4% per landing, though, the remaining x2.76 needs about 85 more landings in 4.5-5.3 days. moe and prefill had zero mechanism landings on 10-30. Decode is byte-capped at 3.3 KB and cannot be diffed against theirs. Full-attention gaps are invisible on the H100 (+7 us there, +59 us on the judge). The remaining riders sit under a 1.2-2% noise floor. And about 4.9 hours of the day were idle under stops, with agent caps of 3-6. A check-only track that starts from the public file takes the architecture gap, the H100 transfer, the clean-room work and the byte cap out of the experiment. It can therefore answer directly whether our learnings improve the record. It cannot move our scored boards: findings must be ported back, or you decide the base question. And since most of our wins re-derived mechanisms their file already has, expect a handful of sub-2% candidates (dequant instruction cuts in their prefill and moe consumers, the unpooler and router push reduce, the faster self-reset), each needing at least 2 checks to read through the noise.

DAYS TO PARITY at 10-30's own rate for each problem:

- moe and prefill never (0 mechanism landings).

- On the aggregate (4.5 days) it looks closer than it is, because 68% of the day's log-gain was attention.

HOW MUCH OF EACH GAP IS STRUCTURE (whole kernel, launch graph, fusion) vs SINGLE KNOBS.
- attention, about 100% structural:
  - The public file is vjeux's, unchanged (PUB/README.md:51).
  - It runs 1 attn_kernel template on all 12 rows; ours runs 2-3 launches on 9 rows (PL/research/dattn-1030.md:50-129,167).
  - The one value test, VT-D16S, was refuted (CL-attention_decode:112), and S3 found 'the gap is in the window kernel body' (CL-attention_decode:133).
- prefill, about 100%: vjeux's file unchanged, and their own variants stayed within noise (PUB/README.md:51-52; PUB/docs/NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:186-189).
- moe, about 91%: their one own decision, KPERM3, is +1.25% of a x1.144 gap (PUB/README.md:62-63). The rest is 'one persistent kernel' (PUB/kernels/muse_moe_w4a16.py:206).
- bf16, about 84%: their own increment is about +2.4% (PUB/docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:64). At equal launch geometry the remaining gap is the 12-bit weight stream (PL/research/bfvt-1030.md:65-69).
- decode, about 100% composite: every row is 1.19-1.35x behind (PL/research/gap-ledger-20261030.txt:2-14), and their own changes netted +0.07% (H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:94).

We leave both out, and their cost to us is unmeasured (AM/ARCH-attn-full.md:99-105,245; AM/ARCH-attn-sliding.md:158-161,242). | Process overhead and stop/start. | - Per-rung and per-check cost: one mechanism per rung (PL/PLAN.md:63-65); an attempt file and checklist per check (RL/PLAN.md:196); a no-code ARCH spec, a review, 0 public reads and an 8-gram audit (AM/PLAN.md:57-73). - Stops: about 13:51Z (PL/LOG.md:4), then 16:05Z to 21:01Z with no new work, 4.9 h (RL/LOG.md:13; AM/PLAN.md:3). Steers at 22:12Z, 22:26Z and 22:30Z (AM/LOG.md:4-17). - Agent cap of 6, then 3 (RL/PLAN.md:22; AM/PLAN.md:117). Only 5 checks were spent in 8.6 h. - Infra: /home filled to 100%, 49 probe links broke, and a scored decode run crashed (AM/LOG.md:59-60,71-73; UC/LOG.md:4-7). - Historic churn: 16 gains from 326 hypotheses, 4.5M tokens per gain (COSTS:53). | medium: limits throughput, not the ceiling |
| There is no working oracle for decode, and the H100 cannot resolve moe. | - The decode shims cannot bind the public file (RL/LANES.tsv:7), and decode inherits the gaps of its components (PL/FINDINGS.md:61). - The moe identity floor is 1-4 us per row (PL/REPLICATION.md:20); node and judge disagree (AM/PLAN.md:89-90). | medium (decode, moe) |
| The knowledge already overlaps: our facts mostly agree with theirs, and most of 10-30's wins re-derived mechanisms their file already has. | - Agreements: PL/REPLICATION.md:15-16; LES:126,197 vs NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:22-27,200-203. - Re-derivations: BF4 = their rgemv, BF5 = their pingpong (CL-bf16_proj:79,82); AT2/AT8/AT5b/S1 = their atomic merge and single launch (PL/research/dattn-1030.md:167). Their moe, decode and prefill already use __expf/__fdividef (grep of PUB/kernels). - So holding the right answer adds little speed. The speed is in code we are not allowed to take. | explains why 'having the answer' did not speed things up |

### Value/decision transfer failures

- VALUE: VT-D16S. Public sliding split count min(32, 264//B) on AT8's keys-per-CTA knob. Their w128 was +0.78..+0.93 us SLOWER at B=16, and w64 was +1.07 us at B=8. Per the record, the d16s gap is per-CTA work, not the grid value (CL-attention_decode:112).
- VALUE: BF-VT. Public sk2 launch values on BF1. The qkv m32 ring at 3 -> 4 stages was x1.112 slower, because 4 x 56 KB of smem leaves about 1 KB of L1. Pooler UPW and unpooler stage changes were null. At equal geometry the gap is their 12-bit stream (PL/research/bfvt-1030.md:58-69; CL-bf16_proj:88).
- SINGLE DECISION: T1 AT2W4. Their 4-warp CTA without their tile chain made d1s +1.90..+1.97 us slower, against a predicted -2.0 us. In our kernel the 8 warps buy latency parallelism (CL-attention_decode:118).
- SINGLE DECISION: AT1. Fixed-reference atomic merge plus last-CTA finalize at sliding verify: +18.6/+18.8 us against a predicted -1.4/-1.7 us. Causes: the one-CTA finalize trap, and the gap sits in the window kernel (PL/LADDER.md:13; CL-attention_decode:89; PL/FINDINGS.md:30-31).
- SINGLE DECISIONS that came out null or slower: - AT6 SEGK boundary charge: null, because our fvg already had the flat split (CL-attention_decode:88). - AT11 two-stream window: +2.0/+0.6 us (CL-attention_decode:94). - AT14 PDL index overlap: about +4 us at B=32 (CL-attention_decode:100). - AT3 single finalize: +0.64/+0.93 us (CL-attention_decode:106). - AT5c DSMEM pre-merge: +1.1/+1.6 us (CL-attention_decode:104). - AT7d/AT7d2 exp2 polynomial: +5.6..+13% math-only (CL-attention_decode:91,109). The public also tried this and lost (PUB/docs/NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:201).
- H100 -> H200 FLIP: BF2, the 12-bit code inside our BF1 ring. It was 0.82-0.88 on the H100 but 1.12-1.29x SLOWER on the judge. Our ring is decode-issue-bound at about 2.7 TB/s, so the byte cut cannot pay at H200 HBM rates (PL/FINDINGS.md:13,68-69; CL-bf16_proj:78,86). BF2b-3 and BF2R were also refuted (CL-bf16_proj:84,90).
- H100 -> H200 FLIP: BF4c-2/3, router fragment-order loads. 0.92-0.95 on the H100 became 1.017 on the judge and was not routed (PL/FINDINGS.md:14; CL-bf16_proj:83). BF4b and BF4c-1 were refuted (CL-bf16_proj:80-81).
- MOE, KPERM and other public moe mechanisms: - The KPERM count gate flipped: we were already below them, at 16.97/16.44 SASS per weight word against their 18.44. The direction still held per SM (x0.90). - Full MO1 needs an x-permute pass that their gather gets for free (PL/REPLICATION.md:18; CL-moe_w4a16:126). - MO1' kp2 read +0.95% on the judge, under the margin (CL-moe_w4a16:127). - The C-moe 2^3 factorial was null (CL-moe_w4a16:129). - MO2 pass order was x0.955-0.963, i.e. slower (CL-moe_w4a16:130). - The MO3-S2 wide twin was +80 us (CL-moe_w4a16:133).
- PREFILL: - PF2 wide tiles were raced on a balanced routing and came out flat on the judge's skewed routing (CL-prefill_layer:82-83; PL/FINDINGS.md:22-24). - PF1 (their cuBLASLt pin direction) and PF3 (about +0.4%) are on the shelf (CL-prefill_layer:81,84). - FA3 replacement was sized out: our rebuilt FA3 is x1.31 slower (CL-prefill_layer:85).
- BUNDLE, CLEAN-ROOM: L3, their persistent moe skeleton around our consume loops. The empty skeleton alone cost 17-19 us: cooperative launch about 5 us, and 2 grid barriers at about 2.5 us each. That is against a 4.1 us gate and against their whole d1 row of 24.64 us. Killed (AM/research/am-moe-s0.md:9-10,56-60; CL-moe_w4a16:135; PUB/RESULTS.md:30). Earlier persistent fusions also lost: EPF x1.09 and UFS x1.03-1.09 (CL-moe_w4a16:110,124).
- BUNDLE, CLEAN-ROOM: F1, a warp-specialized producer for full verify. - It missed at part (a) with +1.5% cycles, and the re-spec came out at +156% cycles because per-row TMA copies are issue-bound (CL-attention_decode:123,126; AM/LOG.md:36-38,53-54). - WGDEC was +3.7..+4.9% on the full kernel (CL-attention_decode:120).
- BUNDLE: S3, per-request split grid plus atomic merge plus slice finalize at v16s/v32s: +5.38/+6.32 us. Per the record, 'the gap is in the window kernel body' (CL-attention_decode:133). The S2 (a+c) bundle was +0.62/+0.66 us (CL-attention_decode:131).
- PORT and ORACLE: - Porting attention's sliding kernels into decode was sized out: it needs AT2/AT8 too, which is more than decode's 3.3 KB of free bytes (CL-decode_layer:111). - The nodejudge decode shims cannot bind the public decode file, so decode cannot be diffed against theirs (RL/LANES.tsv:7).
- HELD BUT UNROUTED or SUB-NOISE: - AT15's import race did not route at its 1.03 bar (CL-attention_decode:108). - S4 (+0.42%) and QMAX (-0.5..-1.25% instructions) held but are under the bar alone (CL-attention_decode:121,129). - The S1 stage 2 judge check came out level at 1.901: v1s improved -1.4 us, which is half of the H100's -2.6 us, and drift on the full rows cancelled it (CL-attention_decode:135).

### What worked

- MECHANISM REPLACEMENT, the atomic-merge family: - AT2 at d1s: 1.7154 -> 1.7571 (CL-attention_decode:101; DEC:472). - AT8 extended to B=2..16: 1.7597 (CL-attention_decode:103; DEC:475). - AT5+AT5b, v1f distributed finalize: 1.8032 (CL-attention_decode:105; DEC:477). - Union AT3b+AT8c: 1.8049 (CL-attention_decode:111; DEC:479). - These were gated by public facts we replicated: R11, held with a guard, and R8, where our self-reset is 1.36 us against their 5.0 (PL/REPLICATION.md:12,17).
- PER-SM and ISSUE-BOUND WORK TRANSFERS, often MORE on the H200: - AT7+AT7c, FP8 PV wgmma: 1.6764 (CL-attention_decode:92; DEC:468). - AT7e: H100 math-only -4% became -6.7/-11.4 us on the judge, 1.6971 (CL-attention_decode:97; PL/FINDINGS.md:11). - AT10+AT12: 714 -> 573 instructions per tile, 1.7154 (CL-attention_decode:98).
- MEASURING THEIR KERNEL BESIDE OURS: - The side-by-side D profile led to AT8F, 1.8264 (CL-attention_decode:113-114,119; DEC:481). - It also showed that sliding rows transfer 1:1 in microseconds from the H100 to the judge (PL/research/dattn-1030.md:40-41).
- A BUNDLE THAT REPLACED A WHOLE KERNEL: arch-mirror S1. - Single launch, in-kernel query prep, one warpgroup per KV head, and every CTA finalizes a slice: 1.8264 -> 1.9027. - This was the largest single landing of the day, +4.2%, against a +1.4% average for the 8 other attention landings (CL-attention_decode:122,130; DEC:482). - S2b, S2d, S4, F2b and S1 stage 2 held on the H100 (CL-attention_decode:124,131,132,134,136).
- bf16 MECHANISMS THAT MATCH THE PUBLIC PER SM: - BF1 sk2 ring: 1.3105 (CL-bf16_proj:77; DEC:465). - BF5 CUTLASS pingpong: judge 0.905-0.909, equal to the public's 0.907 (CL-bf16_proj:79). - BF4 FP32 row GEMV router: 2.4-2.6 us, equal to the public's rgemv at 2.5 us (CL-bf16_proj:82). - BF4d push cluster reduce: judge m1 0.870 (CL-bf16_proj:85). - BF4e router push: 0.866, 1.3425 (CL-bf16_proj:87; DEC:480).
- COMPLIANCE FACT TRANSFER: - R16, taken from their FACTS section 5, found our decode BF16 router input at T<=8 non-compliant (PL/REPLICATION.md:13; CL-decode_layer:108). - The fix cost -3.1% (DEC:467). - Recovery: the DC0b FP32 router race reached 1.6903 (DEC:470), then the DC-AT7e port 1.6989 (DEC:473).
- FACTS THAT AGREE, with no speed attached: - R3 DSMEM: 29.5 GB/s/SM against their ~30. R4 cluster residency: exact match (PL/REPLICATION.md:15-16; PUB/docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:39-40). - Judge noise (LES:12-16,29-31 vs PUB/README.md:42-44). - Side-stream and fork losses (LES:197 vs PUB/docs/NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:22-27). - Full verify is consumer/latency-bound (LES:126 vs NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:200-203). - Split-K L2 write cost at m>=128 (LES:55-56 vs NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:127-130).
- INFORMATIVE DISAGREEMENTS: - Our lesson 'lossless compression is dead' (LES:151) is contradicted by their 12-bit format working inside vjeux's kernel (PUB/README.md:64). It is the kernel, not the codec (CL-bf16_proj:86). - R8: self-reset costs us 1.36 us against their 5.0, in our favour. - R11: their premise is false for our raw FP8 keys, but a guarded fixed reference stays exact (PL/REPLICATION.md:12,17).
- PENDING: the decode fork union checked at 1.7165 against the 1.6989 board (+1.0%). It is blocked only by a master swap-AB prepare hazard that crashed scored run cb182920 (UC branch muse_decode_layer/hypotheses/CLAIMS.md:111-112; UC/LOG.md:4-7).

### What a public-base track fixes / does not fix

A check-only track that starts from the public file (never scored) would remove these problems:
1. The architecture gap leaves the experiment. The base already has the co-designed structure: one attention kernel, one persistent moe kernel, the 12-bit bf16 stream (PUB/README.md:48-52; PUB/kernels/muse_moe_w4a16.py:206; PL/research/dattn-1030.md:50-129). The VT validity test (same work unit, merge and occupancy, no other edit; RL/PLAN.md:101-106) passes trivially, so our findings finally have their bundle to attach to.
2. No H100 -> H200 transfer step. A check runs on the H200 and returns per-row times, so the failure modes in PL/FINDINGS.md:9-17 disappear for screening.
3. No clean-room, ARCH spec, 8-gram audit or bundling argument (AM/PLAN.md:57-73).
4. Byte room. The public decode file is 839,695 B (about 160 KB free) against our 996,666 B (3.3 KB free). The public moe file is 326 KB against our 982 KB.
5. Decode stops inheriting our component gaps (PL/FINDINGS.md:61), and the 'decode cannot be diffed' problem goes away (RL/LANES.tsv:7).
6. Quota is there: 61 checks went unused on 10-30 (UC/LOG.md:36).
7. It answers your question directly: does anything we learned beat the record, measured on the record itself.

The same track would NOT fix these:
1. Our scored boards do not move.
   - A finding proven on their base must be ported back to ours, which is the same transfer problem in reverse (PUB/docs/NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:101; LES:157).
   - The alternative is to change the scored base, which reverses your standing rule (PL/PLAN.md:19-25,58-62). That is your decision.
2. Noise.
   - Our candidates are 0.2-1% each. Their own noise call is 2% (PUB/README.md:42-44), and an unchanged file spread 1.6% (H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:61).
   - Each arm needs at least 2 checks (NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:236).
   - Each problem also needs at least 2 identity checks of their unchanged file to re-baseline. Their published numbers are single runs from 10-29 20:54Z (PUB/README.md:25).
   - These checks share the 20 per problem per day cap with the scored track (rules/technical-rules.llms.txt:102).
3. Little of what we know is new to their file.
   - Most 10-30 wins re-derived mechanisms they already have.
   - What is genuinely ours:
     - dequant instruction cuts: 16.97 against their 18.44 SASS per word (PL/REPLICATION.md:18), and prefill A3 at 26 -> 18 per k16 (CL-prefill_layer:74);
     - last-CTA self-reset at 1.36 us against their 5.0 (PL/REPLICATION.md:17);
     - the unpooler m<=8 cluster split-K with push reduce (gap-ledger-20261030.txt:26,40; CL-bf16_proj:85);
     - the router push reduce (CL-bf16_proj:87);
     - QMAX and AT8F-style entry hoists;
     - the T=128 hot/cold fork (UC branch decode CLAIMS.md:112).
4. Their prepare clock is already near 300 s (H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:67), so in-prepare races are tight.
5. The track inherits the precision choices we ruled out: the lazy offset and the E4M3 row sum (AM/ARCH-attn-full.md:245). That is acceptable for a non-scored check, but anything ported back must drop them.
6. Rights and rules.
   - The never-send guard (AM/PLAN.md:113-115; RL/PLAN.md:119) has to be lifted explicitly by you.
   - The Apache-2.0 NOTICE and Nathen Fernandes' MIT license text must stay in the file (PUB/README.md:67-68,106; rules/official-rules.txt:259).
7. The H100 still cannot screen moe or full-attention rows. Judge checks become the only oracle, so throughput is limited by checks, not GPUs.

## muse_attention_decode

101 mechanisms. Relations: public_does_differently 28, not_comparable 26, public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation 18, public_does_same 15, public_negative_agrees 12, public_lacks 2

### Public file facts

CITATION KEYS: PUB = /home/user/git/okc-muse-spark-kernels/kernels/muse_attention_decode.py (alygg77@6cae49a); CLAIMS = /home/user/git/musespark/muse_attention_decode/hypotheses/CLAIMS.md on master 3d4f06b2; AM-CLAIMS = campaign/arch-mirror:muse_attention_decode/hypotheses/CLAIMS.md (lines 120-136 are branch-only; campaign/union-confirm adds nothing); PL = /home/user/git/musespark/.worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-30-public-ladder; AM = /home/user/git/musespark/.worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror.

SIZE AND IDENTITY: 154,807 bytes, 3,182 lines. sha256 e6d929d40e6c... (results.json:802). Judge job 1c8d81a0dbd94ee0, 2.6530x [2.6132, 2.6928] (RESULTS.md:78). For comparison, our master file is 694,119 B and uc/attn is 756,323 B; okc refuses files over 1,000,000 B.

HEADER: PUB:1 '# okc: problem=muse_attention_decode'. PUB:2 'Portions Copyright vjeux ... (commit f35a41e) ... Apache License 2.0'. PUB:3 'Submitted by alygg77 under the Apache License 2.0 with this notice retained'. NOTICE:1-28 says the file is vjeux's, submitted unchanged.

BUILD: one load_inline module, 'okc_attn_v298_90a', flags -O3 -gencode=arch=compute_90a,code=sm_90a --expt-relaxed-constexpr (PUB:2947-2959). It is built lazily on the first kernel_fn call via _module() (PUB:2962-2966), not at import. There is no prepare, no import-time race and no self-check. About 20 kernel instantiations: attn_kernel<L,false,MODE> for MODE in {1,2,3,5,7,9,11,13,15} plus attn_kernel<L,true>, for L in {1,8} (PUB:2769-2773, 2783). nvcc time was never measured by us; our own file imports in 303-314 s against a 480 s clock.

TUNED TABLE: _TUNED (PUB:2940-2943) has (32,8,True,1) -> n_splits=4, nc=1, coop=1 (v32s) and (1,1,False,1) -> nc=8, coop=1, n_splits=252 (d1f). Everything else comes from _plan (PUB:3014-3063).

ROUTES PER SCORED ROW (these match the dattn grids, PL/research/dattn-1030.md:47-129):
- d1s: 32 CTAs x 128 threads; one-tile build + length speculation; nc=1; last-CTA finalize.
- d16s: 16 splits x 16 requests x 128 threads.
- d32s: 8 x 32 x 128 threads.
- v1s: 33 x 256 threads; one-tile + speculation + cooperative finalize.
- v16s: 8 x 16 x 256 threads, cooperative.
- v32s: 4 x 32 x 256 threads, cooperative (tuned).
- d1f: 252 x 128 threads, nc=8, cooperative, speculation.
- v1f: 252 x 256 threads, nc=8, cooperative, speculation.
- d16f/d32f: flat, 264 x 256 threads (128 producer + 128 consumer), not cooperative.
- v16f/v32f: flat, 264 x 384 threads (128 producer + 256 consumer), cooperative.
PDL is compiled but OFF on the scored path (pdl=None, PUB:3100, 3182). Clusters are compiled but unreachable for q_len 1/8 (every branch returns before PUB:3049). The exp-phase alternation and two-stream code survive as comments only (PUB:242-243 unused, 1771-1773, 1946, 2160-2163). The PyTorch fallback _fallback (PUB:3135-3168) covers unsupported shapes.

PER-US HEADLINE VALUE ON PUB (load = 46 x sliding + 16 x full; headline = geomean over 6 loads; gain = (L/(L-w))^(1/6) - 1): d1s 1.983%/us, v1s 1.497, d1f 0.659, v1f 0.503, d16s 0.458, v16s 0.363, d32s 0.257, v32s 0.215, d16f 0.158, v16f 0.125, d32f 0.089, v32f 0.074.

NO ROW WHERE OURS BEATS THEIRS: our judge rows are slower than PUB's on all 12 shapes, in both BEST 47b125ee (1.9027) and union check 32feaefa (1.927): ours/theirs 1.17x (d32f) to 1.78x (d32s). So the strongest graft class (a row we win) is empty.

WHAT AN OKC CHECK OF A PUB-BASE VARIANT NEEDS:
(1) the user's explicit lift of the never-send guard for a separate, never-scored track;
(2) G0a + G0b applied, because the unmodified file fails the user's FA3-precision rule; and since their bytes cannot be sent unmodified, the comparison baseline must be public-C (PUB + G0), not 2.653;
(3) PUB:1-3 kept, plus an Apache-2.0 modification notice and a design citation (~150 B, AM/PLAN.md:53);
(4) nothing else: no weights or prepare, and 155 KB is far under the byte limit.
A same-cluster pair (public-C vs public-C + graft) is needed, because judge noise is about ±1.5% and clusters differ by 0.4-1% (README.md:56-59; LESSONS.md:29-31).

### Public compliance flags

- Under the user's AT7c rule (FA3 semantics), q_len-8 rows sum the softmax denominator from E4M3-ROUNDED P via V^T ones rows on the PV tensor core (m64n72k32): PUB:551, 1062-1063, 1838-1843, 1948-1949, 2002-2003, 2072, 2118-2119. This is the AT7b variant our repo left out (PL/FINDINGS.md:43-44; CLAIMS:90; at16-1030.md:29-30; AM/ARCH-attn-full.md:102-105). It affects v1s, v16s, v32s, v1f, v16f and v32f.
- q_len-8 rows use a lazy softmax offset: offset = running max (P <= 1 at the max), with a rescale only when a score passes it by 8 (PUB:1700-1711, 1921-1945; packed-P test 1901-1916, 1963-1988, 2011-2053). FA3 uses Max_offset 8 (P_max = 256). So PUB's E4M3 P keeps up to 8 fewer binades at the low end: keys below ~2^-9 of the running max round to 0 where FA3 keeps them down to ~2^-17. That is below SGLang's precision under our rule (AT7f, CLAIMS:95; AM/ARCH-attn-sliding.md:158-161), and a reviewer may read it against rules:137-139 ('no skipping values by their attention weight'). Fix: G0a.
- The fixed-reference merge has no guard: f = ex2(fminf(m,126)) (PUB:2172, 2204), relying on 'QK-norm bounds the logits' (PUB:27-28). Our R11 found that premise false because cache keys are raw FP8 (PL/REPLICATION.md:12). It holds empirically on the judge's generator (row max <= 65.4 log2). This is a correctness risk on held-out or nearby inputs, not a precision-rule breach.
- Length speculation from the page-table width (B==1, page 1): PUB:1284-1293, 2473-2524, 2808-2811. It is exact (verified, re-runs on a miss), and the user approved this class 2026-10-30 22:04Z (AM/LOG.md:43-46). On nearby shapes with a wider table it always misses (technical-rules.llms.txt:112 'much slower on nearby shapes'); our miss cost was +0.17..0.24 us (AM-CLAIMS:136).
- Process, not a rule: our never-send guard forbids sending their file or anything derived from it to okc check/submit (AM/PLAN.md:113-115; campaigns/2026-10-30-rank-ladder/PLAN.md:119), and the clean-room rule forbids using their code as a base (AM/PLAN.md:39-40, 55-71). The user's new request asks for exactly that as a separate check-only track, so the guard must be lifted explicitly for that track only. Agents who read or edit PUB must not then write master's attention kernels (clean-room contamination). Apache-2.0 §4 requires keeping PUB:1-3 and adding a notice that the file was modified.
- Robustness: the cooperative finalize uses a plain launch plus an occupancy query (no cudaLaunchCooperativeKernel), with spin waits that __trap after 2^24 polls (PUB:132-134, 2252-2286, 2386-2419, 2795-2823). It is safe under the judge's serialized graph replays; it deadlocks or traps if CTAs are not co-resident.
- Note only: cross-call scratch state (workspace, epoch and sense counters at ws_cnt offsets 8192/12288/24576/30000-30022; PUB:1265-1266, 2253-2264, 2386-2398, 2527-2528, 2982-2992) is self-reset scratch, not data-derived caching (TASK.md:97), so it is OK. _TUNED overrides for exactly two scored shapes, 'measured on the judge's H200' (PUB:2938-2943), are allowed shape branching; nearby shapes fall back to _plan. Fast-approximation math (ex2.approx, __expf/__fdividef sigmoid, __sincosf with Cody-Waite reduction, MUFU.RCP for 1/k_scale; PUB:253-257, 436-440, 1345-1352, 1501-1503) is FP32-level, as in our audit (PL/research/compliance-audit-1030.md:87-104).

### Graft-onto-public candidates and verifier verdicts

| id | verdict | our finding | public target | predicted (inventory) | corrected (verifier) | verifier evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G0a-fa3-maxoffset8 | **survives** | Our P range rule (AT7, FA3's Max_offset 8) and the AT7f refusal of an approximate max. On PUB: start and move the offset at (running max - 8) instead of the max. Keep the same 'P > 256' test (byte > 0x78), which then fires on every new maximum. This gives FA3's exact P range of (0, 256] with PUB's packed-P structure intact. Under i.i.d. scores that happens on roughly 1/3 to 1/2 of a flat segment's 76-152 tiles. ### Candidates the verifier added

- No row-level graft exists in the 'ours beats theirs' direction. On every one of the 12 rows our judge time is slower than PUB's, both in BEST (BEST.result.json shapes[0..11]) and in the latest union c05b8c33 (c05b8c33:12-23 vs RESULTS:95-106). The only mechanisms of ours that PUB lacks are W2 (G1), the R11 guard (G0c) and the FA3-semantics softmax (G0a/G0b, which are costs).
- M1, a cheaper compliant G0a (not measured). Start the offset at the first-tile max - 8 and trigger the repack on the satfinite top byte 0x7E (P >= 432) instead of > 0x78. P_max then stays in [256,448), so the low end is at or below FA3's (PL/research/at7-1030.md:46) and nothing clips (PL/research/at7fg-1030.md:21-22). A rescale fires only when a row max rises >= ~0.75 log2 above its anchor, not on every new record. This keeps PUB's rare-rescale structure (PUB:1963-1988) at close to PUB's cost. It is the variant to price 'public-C' with.
- M2, a diagnostic for PUB's held-out-data risk (correctness only, H100-runnable, no timing). LESSONS:145 shows that P <= 1 with no offset broke the judge tolerance on coherent long-context data (rel L2 0.107 vs 0.088). PUB's lazy offset reaches that case whenever a flat segment's first tile holds the segment max (PUB:1921-1928). Run PUB's file through reference.check_implementation on coherent full-verify inputs that plant each segment's max in its first tile. This tells whether the 'proven' file has tolerance margin under the final review's reruns (AGENTS.md: 'held-out data too').
- M3, speculative, sign unknown: 8 warps at PUB's d1s (warp = one KV head x 16 keys, 4-way key merge in place of PUB:2126-2154), for the d1s ONE+SPEC build only (PUB:2808-2811). Our most repeated d1s result is that latency parallelism beats instruction count. T1 found 4 warps +1.9 us vs 8 (CLAIMS:118). S2 ac, which is PUB's exact 4-warp + pairwise-merge layout, still lost +0.62 us despite -21% instructions (AM-CLAIMS:131). PUB's d1s runs 32 CTAs at 6.8% warps active (dattn-1030.md:138). Predict -0.6..+0.3 us at 1.852%/us. Prescreen on the H100 (d1s transfers 1:1, dattn-1030.md:27).

### Public mechanisms carrying the unmet gains

| Two one-request sliding rows in single-launch builds. d1s: 4 warps (KV head x key half), a pairwise key-half merge, then a 'one tile per split' build that removes the loop, in-loop copies and rescale at compile time. v1s: the same single-tile build with a cooperative slice finalize. Both use length speculation (page-table width) so the page indices go out with the length load. Every entry load goes in one trip, and prep is hidden under the first gather. | d1s, v1s | d1s 8.03 / v1s 10.77 (BEST 47b125ee); 6.96 / 9.27 (check 32feaefa) | d1s 5.19 / v1s 6.77 | From BEST: d1s 12.0% + v1s 12.0% = 24% of the log gap. Matching both = +4.03% + 4.03%, about +8.2% headline (d1s 1.296%/us x 2.84 us; v1s 0.908%/us x 4.00 us on our loads). After union check 32feaefa: d1s 8.3% + v1s 8.3%. | PUB:2473-2524 (length speculation and retry), 1226-1338 (entry and the single-tile copies at 1300-1323), 1564-1661 (q_len-1 math; single-tile skips rescale at 1637), 2126-2154 (key-half merge), 2242-2318 (cooperative finalize), 2769-2811 (build selection), 3032-3040 (plan) | Done: AT2, AT8F, AT3/AT3b/AT3c, AT8G, T1, S1 (landed), S1 stage 2, S1d, S2 b/ac/e/d (CLAIMS:101,106,110,111,116,118,119,120; AM-CLAIMS:128,131,132,135,136). ### Verifier corrections to the unmet-gains list

- Per-us rates are off. PUB's loads are decode_1 = 46*5.19+16*10.95 = 413.9 and verify_1 = 539.4, with rate = w/(6*load). That gives d1s 1.852%/us (not 1.983), v1s 1.421 (not 1.497), d1f 0.644 (not 0.659), v1f 0.494 (not 0.503) and d16s 0.450 (not 0.458). The 'our loads' parentheticals (d1s 1.296, v1s 0.908) should be 1.240 and 0.880 at BEST, and they do not reproduce the stated +4.03%. Those +4.03% figures and all the share percentages are correct only via the exact log formula.
- The ledger is stale. The latest union check c05b8c33 (1.958x) measured d1s 7.12, d1f 15.53, d16s 10.33, d16f 98.98, d32s 14.74, d32f 179.99, v1s 9.14, v1f 23.82, v16s 19.03, v16f 132.36, v32s 25.71, v32f 243.48 (c05b8c33:12-23). Remaining log gap 0.294. Re-ranked shares: v32f 12.2%, v1f 12.0%, v16f 10.8%, d1s 9.5%, v1s 8.3%, d1f 7.7%, d16f 7.2%, v16s 6.7%, v32s 5.9%, d32f 5.4%, d32s 3.8%, d16s 3.1%. d1s+v1s is 17.8% now, not 24%, and is no longer the top bundle.
- d32s: 'entered union check c05b8c33, result pending (AM-CLAIMS:129)' is wrong. AM-CLAIMS:129 never mentions c05b8c33. c05b8c33 measured d32s 14.74 us, and 985ae65c (union + S4) 14.93, against 17.46 in 32feaefa (commit 0bc04b48). S4 runs as a single kernel with no pre-attention (c05b8c33 profile 4: sliding_at2_kernel 13.2 us). The gap is +5.0 us, not +7.55.
- d16f/d32f: the stale our_us hides that F2 part (b) is already in c05b8c33. 07a5e7f1 merges 8b24ed69 (F2 b in splitkv_attn_sk_kernel + QMAX). It measured 98.98 / 179.99 against 105.17 / 189.65 in same-session 985ae65c, i.e. -6.2 / -9.7 us on the judge from an H100 change of -1.98% / -0.93% kernel us and -8.7% warp-insts (AM-CLAIMS:134). That is about 3x the H100 transfer. The gap is now +15.3 / +20.1 us, not +19.5 / +26.9.
- v16f/v32f, why_not_transferred (2), is false. F1 stage 2 (a) did build PUB's producer type: threads 256-383, cp.async 16 B with 8 lanes per 128-B row, cp.async.mbarrier.arrive.noinc with 128 arrivals, 384 threads, setmaxnreg 24/104 (am/f1:campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror/research/am-f1.md:17-22; AM-CLAIMS:123). It was killed on H100 m5 cycles (+1.5%) and consumer insts (+1.7%), with mbarrier polls adding ~4M insts (am-f1.md:53-56). The miss was the H100 oracle plus the wait scheme, not 'the wrong producer'. Also in (3): the 32-FADD row-sum figure is at16-1030.md:8 / :29-30, not 'AT7f CLAIMS:95' (that line is about the max).
- v16s/v32s: 'PUB fences once per CTA; ours used per-thread release fences (CLAIMS:89)' is not supported. AT1 was built with one per-CTA release (PL/research/at1-1030.md:50, 110-112). S3 then reproduced the atomics cost (+8.7 / +9.4 us) on a PUB-like per-request grid and blamed red volume plus a serial last-arrival finalize (am/s3:campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror/research/am-s3.md:70, 78-79). Its cooperative finalize (c) recovered -3.9 / -3.4. Both conclude the gap is the window-kernel body. Minor: whole-tile chunking makes v16s chunks 320 keys and leaves the 8th split empty, so 7x16 = 112 CTAs are active (PUB:2485-2503), not 8x16.
- d16s: '2 tiles per CTA through a refilled ring' is wrong. With non-flat STAGES = 3 (PUB:151) and PRE = 2 (PUB:1295), both tiles are issued in the prologue (PUB:1324-1331), and the in-loop refill 'i + STAGES - 1 < ntiles' (PUB:1583) never fires at 2 tiles. PUB's d16s is one gather group. S2 e (+0.96 us, AM-CLAIMS:131) therefore tested a phantom, just as F1 e did with the exp alternation (PUB:242-243 never called; that part of the agent's text is correct).
- v1f: 'T3 (ring 3->4 with 252 CTAs) was proposed and never run' is pre-refuted by PUB's own NEG:210-213: a 4-stage ring was one of 11 lost arms at d1f/v1f.
- Cross-cutting single launch: d32s no longer pays a pre-attention launch in c05b8c33 (S4), so the 'about 2.9%' should drop d32s's 2.0 us; v1f's pre-attention there is 1.5 us. 'Fusion placed ahead of the gather failed (CLAIMS:46, 59, 66, 110)' is also inaccurate for CLAIMS:59, which failed correctness at B=2..16 rather than on placement.

### Why the gap persists here (inventory agent)

Headline gap: 2.653 / 1.903 = 1.394x (log 0.332 over 6 loads). Union check 32feaefa closes it to 1.927 (1.363x on the same-run baselines). Ours is slower on all 12 rows. Every positive mechanism we measured (AT2, AT8F, AT10/AT12, AT7e, S1, S2 b/d, F2 b, S4) is a mechanism the public file already has, so the day mostly re-derived their file piece by piece. These are the reasons, with evidence:

(1) Architecture, not mechanism. Their decisions are co-adapted, and eight times one of them lost when moved alone onto our CTA: T1 4-warp +1.9 us (CLAIMS:118); VT-D16S w128 +0.8 (CLAIMS:112); S2 ac +0.62 and S2 e +0.96 (AM-CLAIMS:131); AT1 atomics +18.6 (CLAIMS:89); S3 +5.4/+6.3 (AM-CLAIMS:133); AT8b neutral (CLAIMS:102); F1(d) ring 4 failed (AM-CLAIMS:123). The arch-mirror PLAN says so itself: values failed 2/2 and single decisions failed (AM/PLAN.md:27-33). Bundles (S1, S2 b) are what landed.

(2) The oracle cannot see the biggest block. Full rows v16f/v32f/d16f/d32f carry 33% of the log gap (BEST shares 8.8/10.3/7.9/6.1%). On the H100 both files are HBM-bound and nearly tie (v32f +7.0 us there vs +59 on the judge; PUB gains 1.45x from H100 to H200, ours 1.12x; dattn-1030.md:36-44). So F1, WGDEC and AT16 were judged on a machine where their effect was invisible, and the fvg math-only proxy (m7) is only a proxy.

(3) Compliance by policy. Two of PUB's q_len-8 mechanisms are excluded by the user's FA3-precision rule: the rounded-P row sum (32 FADD/tile saved) and the lazy offset (~28 instr/tile saved). Together they are ~60 of the ~83 instr/tile difference behind 113.2M vs 86.5M warp-insts at v32f (attnfull-1030.md:31; at16-1030.md:29-30; AT7f CLAIMS:95). Length speculation was held for a ruling until 22:04Z. Part of the 'proven record' is therefore unreachable for us by choice, and the fair target is a compliant public-C (estimated 2.55-2.65, unmeasured).

(4) Mis-mirroring through the no-code ARCH. Our producer attempts built the wrong producer: one bulk-copy warp (REWRITE_READ:165-197, 0.49 TB/s) and per-row TMA copies (AM-CLAIMS:126, +156% cycles). PUB uses 128 threads issuing 16 B cp.async with cp.async.mbarrier.arrive.noinc (PUB:999-1106). ARCH-attn-full.md:79-81 also described an exp-phase alternation that PUB does not execute (PUB:242-243 never called; comments at 1771-1773), and F1(e) spent a build on it (+7.7%). The details that decide the result (copy shape, single-tile builds, fence placement before the reds) are the ones a no-code spec drops.

(5) Partial transfer. Sliding rows move about 1:1, but even there S1 stage 2's -2.6 us predicted on the H100 became -1.4 us on the judge (AM-CLAIMS:135), and unchanged full rows drifted +0.9..+2.7 us in the same run and cancelled the gain (check 938c13fe 1.901 vs board 1.903).

(6) Noise vs rung size. Single runs drift 1.5-2.5% per shape (LESSONS.md:12-15); the churn margin is 1.24% (LESSONS.md:29-31); the board 1.634 was a lucky draw (CLAIMS:64). Most rungs were worth 0.2-1% (AT8c, AT10, AT15, QMAX, S4 = 0.42%), so they piled up as shelf and leads until unions (32feaefa 1.927, c05b8c33 pending) were coalesced at the end of the day.

(7) Layering cost. 8 of 12 rows still pay a separate pre-attention launch (1.6-4.1 us; about 2.9% headline if all were removed), and 2 rows run 3 kernels. Our file is 694-756 KB with 303-314 s of import-time races against a 480 s clock and a 1 MB cap; PUB is 155 KB with static plan tables and no races. Every new variant costs us bytes and import time and needs a gate that can flip (CLAIMS:64).

(8) Misleading early refutations steered effort away. CLAIMS:67 sized the d16f/d32f rewrite out as near floor, yet PUB is 19.5/26.9 us faster there. CLAIMS:61 read the full-decode merge as not the bottleneck, but F2 step 0 found the gap in the tail (23.4k vs 8.3k cycles, AM-CLAIMS:134). Fusion-ahead-of-gather refutations (CLAIMS:46, 59, 66, 110) hid the working order (prep after the first gather) until S1.

Bottom line for the new track: few of our findings can speed up the public file. The real grafts are compliance hardening (G0a/G0b/G0c, which cost rather than gain), W2 copies at v1s (+0.4..0.85%, low confidence), and cheap plan-knob sweeps that the H100 can prescreen on sliding rows. Their main value is to measure how much of 2.653 survives our precision rule, and that measured number is the target our own file should be steered toward.

### Verifier: inventory misclassifications

- at7b-rounded-p-rowsum: tagged public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation, but this was a compliance exclusion and never a speed refutation (PL/FINDINGS.md:36-38; PL/research/at16-1030.md:29-30). PUB's speed cannot contradict a rule. It should read 'public uses a mechanism we excluded on compliance'.
- at7f-rowmax-cut: wrong relation, and a different mechanism. AT7f refused an approximate max because satfinite clips P above 448 (CLAIMS:95; PL/research/at7fg-1030.md:21-22). PUB's lazy offset is not an approximate max and never clips: it rescales at P > 256 (PUB:1963-1988). Its risk is the opposite, low-end underflow (LESSONS:145). It should be classed as compliance-gated and unruled (AM/ARCH-attn-full.md:104-105), not as contradicting.
- at6-segk-charge: consistent, not contradicted. PUB prices SEGK for its own boundary cost of about 7 us for q staging, refill and merge (PUB:2655-2657). AT6's null was on a ring without per-request refill, and its reopen condition names exactly PUB's case (CLAIMS:88).
- at8b-atomic-d32s: consistent and since superseded. CLAIMS:102 refuted the merge as the d32s lever and said it 'needs a gather change or pre-attn fusion'. PUB keeps the same merge, and our S4 (same split + atomics plus in-kernel prep) later took judge d32s from 17.46 to 14.74 us (c05b8c33:16; AM-CLAIMS:124).
- vt-d16s-public-split: consistent. CLAIMS:112 concluded 'the d16s gap is per-CTA work, not the grid value'. That PUB's value works inside PUB's 4-warp, 58 KB CTA (dattn-1030.md:64) agrees with it.
- am-s2e-refilled-ring-d16s: misclassified. PUB does NOT refill at d16s: 2 tiles per CTA go out together in the 3-stage prologue (PUB:151, 1295, 1324-1331), and PUB:1583 never fires. The public matches our refutation of the refilled ring.
- ws-bulk-producer-onelaunch: consistent. PUB avoided exactly the refuted variant: no bulk per-row K/V copies, a 16 B cp.async producer at 2 CTAs/SM (PUB:1077-1106), and bulk copies only for page indices at q_len 1 (PUB:1010-1036).
- am-f1-producer-wg: the relation stands but the evidence is wrong. F1 stage 2 built PUB's producer type (am/f1:campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror/research/am-f1.md:17-22). The contradiction comes from judging full rows on H100 m5 cycles, which do not transfer (dattn-1030.md:40-44). It does not come from bulk vs cp.async.
- at1-atomic-merge-persistent-sw: the relation can stand, but the public_evidence implies PUB wins by 'no per-thread fences'. AT1 also used one per-CTA release (PL/research/at1-1030.md:50, 110), and S3 reproduced the cost on a PUB-like grid (AM-CLAIMS:133).
- sd-s3-fused-preattn and sv1-pseudo-fused-preattn: better classed as superseded by our own landings than as a live public contradiction. CLAIMS:59 failed correctness at B=2..16. In-kernel prep then landed via AT2/AT8/AT8F (CLAIMS:101, 103, 119), S1 (CLAIMS:120) and S4 (c05b8c33: d32s single kernel).

## muse_decode_layer

75 mechanisms. Relations: public_does_differently 25, public_does_same 16, not_comparable 15, public_negative_agrees 8, public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation 7, public_negative_contradicts 2, public_lacks 2

### Public file facts

Path /home/user/git/okc-muse-spark-kernels/kernels/muse_decode_layer.py (alygg77@6cae49a). 839,695 B, 13,887 lines, sha256 8f17613bafb23691... (matches results.json kernel_sha256, judge job a61afb9687f14eff, 2.2058 [2.1727, 2.2389]). It leaves 160,305 B under the 1,000,000 B cap; ours is 996,666 B on master and 998,609-998,851 B on uc/dec. Line 1 is the '# okc: problem=muse_decode_layer' marker; lines 2-6 are the attribution header (Matsvei context bind and cuBLASLt pins, vjeux f35a41e Apache-2.0, alygg77 Apache-2.0); NOTICE gives the credits. No nvcc runs at import; the only import-time work is the Python _stored_pos (line 6782). In prepare: 10 builds in parallel threads, '~85 s of nvcc' (10926: _att7, 4 moe builds, bfp ext, graft glue/b2 and sl_cluster through tvm_ffi), then more builds in later prepare overrides: _de_ext (12970), _router_fp32_ext (13182), _gle_ext (13541), _gle_b1x (13841), and per-T moe builds for T in (1,8,16,32,128,256) (13123-13128). Then the _tune graph timings (10745-10881), the chain-policy race (12315-12347), the attention self-test (11090-11114) and the sentinel guard check (11082-11088). The file is a stack of redefinitions: prepare is redefined about 10 times, _after_attention about 8 times and kernel_fn 3 times, and the last definition wins. Final T=1 path, 8 launches all PDL: sk2 qkv, ATT7 attention, gle_o (o + boundary-1 epilogue), gle_pr (12-bit pooler + FP32 router), _gle_route, TINY moe, sk2 sliced unpooler, _sl_cluster_finish. Tuned tables: PIN_PICK 102-103, _TUNED7 3748-3756, MOE_*_DEFINES 6629-6635, bfp_SK2_CONFIGS/PICK 9223-9250, BFP_PDL_N=2 9852, _GRAFT_LT_PICKS (hard-coded cuBLASLt algo words) 11974-11985, _GRAFT_QKV256 11986, SL_CLUSTER_CONFIG 12663, SL_ROUTE_CONFIG 12798, SL_TILE_CONFIG 12869, GLE_* 13238-13242, OKC_* attention macros 124-253 (OKC_PDLKV 0 at 168). Dependencies: triton gluon (11322-11323), tvm_ffi, libcublasLt.so.13 and libcuda.so.1 through ctypes, sgl_kernel flash_attn only as a fallback. For an okc check it needs nothing beyond the file. Our rules require a modification notice in the header (Apache-2.0 4b) and the user's explicit lift of the never-send rule. For H100 prescreens, their timing_kit/paired.py (8 captures, random 0-32 MiB pads, 48 ABBA rounds) can drive their prepare/kernel_fn in-process, which avoids the nodejudge shims that blocked D-decode (campaigns/2026-10-30-rank-ladder/LANES.tsv:7). Docs: docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md and docs/NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md; per-row judge times in RESULTS.md.

### Public compliance flags

- Softmax denominator from rounded P: at q_len 8 the PV wgmma reads 8 extra V^T rows of e4m3 ones, so the row sum is the sum of e4m3-rounded P (kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:704-705, 1970, 2238-2240). Our rule left exactly this out as below SGLang precision (AT7b: campaigns/2026-10-30-public-ladder/FINDINGS.md:37; arch-mirror LOG.md:27). It must be ruled on, or replaced with an FP32 row sum, before any send.
- Lazy max offset: OKC_LAZYMAX 1 with OKC_PCHK (kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:164, 242, 2328-2359) skips per-tile row-max shuffles and moves the offset only on a threshold. Our arch-mirror left out the 'lazy offset' (LOG.md:27). R11 found their logit-bound premise false for raw FP8 keys, though a guarded fallback is exact (campaigns/2026-10-30-public-ladder/REPLICATION.md:12). Gray area.
- Comment contradicts the code at kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:11136-11140. It says FA3 keeps every q_len 8 shape because their q_len-8 query scaling rounds coarser than FA3 at kv_scale 1/64 (1.41x SGLang's error on conformance test 9). Yet _supported7 (11042-11046) routes q_len 8 to their kernel. OKC_Q8RAW (161) may be the fix. Verify q_len-8 accuracy on nearby shapes with kv_scale != 1 before sending (rules/technical-rules.llms.txt:112 nearby-shape review).
- Four CUDA modules compile with tvm_ffi.cpp.load_inline, not PyTorch's load_inline (kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:11769-11770, 11778-11779, 12633-12636, 13842-13844). The rules say to compile native CUDA with PyTorch's load_inline (rules/technical-rules.llms.txt:96). The judge accepted the file (job a61afb96), but this is a rule-text gray area.
- Raw ctypes library use: libcublasLt.so.13 with hard-coded algorithm words (kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:11905-11985), and libcuda.so.1 context binding on every kernel_fn call (12995-13040). Not forbidden, but it depends on the library version. Plans are skipped if AlgoCheck fails.
- At T=1 the pooler runs on bf16(u) and bf16(v), two BF16 rows per token, then recombines in FP32 with the deferred norm (kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:13229-13237, 13596-13608). SGLang feeds bf16(h). The activation stays BF16 but is rounded at a different point. Low-risk gray area under TASK.md:103.
- Prepare-time budget is unmeasured on our side: ~85 s of parallel nvcc (kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:10926), at least 5 more load_inline builds (12970-12981, 13182-13219, 13541-13560, 13841-13846, per-T moe builds 13123-13128), _tune graph timings over 9 token counts (10745-10881), and a 6x5x32 chain-policy race (12315-12347), all against the 300 s limit (rules/technical-rules.llms.txt:98). The public saw a 311.9 s moe prepare (docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:67).
- Process, not rules: checking their file, even modified, breaks our standing rules: never a base (campaigns/2026-10-30-public-ladder/PLAN.md:133-135), the never-send guard (campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror/PLAN.md:113-115), and 'never get judge profiles by checking their file' (campaigns/2026-10-30-rank-ladder/PLAN.md:119). The user's explicit lift is needed. Apache-2.0 section 4(b) requires a prominent modification notice; keep header lines 1-6 (vjeux, Matsvei, alygg77).
- Checked and clean: no setattr, torch.backends edits, subprocess, socket or frame access (grep found nothing). The FP32 router at T<=8 is compliant and pinned (kernels/muse_decode_layer.py:13878-13887). FP8 PV with e4m3 P (154) matches SGLang's FA3 FP8 path, as in our AT7 rules reading.

### Candidates the verifier added

- Rows where our judge time beats theirs: none. This graft class is empty. - Decode: all 12 rows are 1.18-1.38x slower (muse_decode_layer/hypotheses/BEST.result.json dfb318ee vs RESULTS.md). - Attention: 1.17-1.78x slower. MoE: 1.08-1.22x slower. - bf16: ties at best. o m1 5.32 vs 5.24, router m1 3.71 vs 3.69, unpooler m128 19.09 vs 19.07, o m256 9.28 vs 9.26 us (muse_bf16_proj BEST.result.json). - research/gap-ledger-20261030.txt:25 and :40 seem to show ours faster (4.9/5.3 vs 5.2/5.5 us). Those rows are o rows mislabelled 'unpooler', from an older bf16 run.
- R11 merge guard (send safety, not speed). - The public's split-KV merge rescales to a fixed 2^0 reference with ex2(fminf(m_r, 126.f)) (pub :2634, :2683, :2715). That assumes a logit bound. - REPLICATION.md:12 found the bound false for raw FP8 keys. It only holds on judge cases (row max <= 65.4 log2). - Our R=16 reference plus a per-segment LSE fallback is exact for any input. That matters for the nearby-shape review (rules/technical-rules.llms.txt:112) of any public-derived file. - Cost: about one compare per row.
- Lazy-max headroom variant (pairs with G7). - Set the lazy offset to the row max minus 7 instead of the row max (pub :2348), and move it only when P would pass the e4m3 limit of 448 (:2393). - This keeps most of OKC_LAZYMAX's saved shuffles and gives FA3-like e4m3 tail range. - Unmeasured. It needs a compliance ruling and an H200 cost read on v16f/v32f before it can replace a full removal.

### Verifier corrections to the unmet-gains list

- #1 MoE T>=16, log-gap basis: - The decode board is 1.6989 (muse_decode_layer/hypotheses/BEST.json:5). The log gap is ln(2.2058/1.6989) = 0.261, or 0.270 summed per row on dfb318ee's row times. No 1.740 board exists. - The row times used (d16s 162.0, d32s 187.1, v16s 300.5, v32s 402.6) differ from the board result (164.9 / 190.5 / 312.8 / 401.4 us in BEST.result.json). Name the source run.
- #1 d32 cut is overstated: - The assumed -20 us is 2.0-2.4x the measured MoE-board gap at d32: ours 132.3-134.2 vs theirs 123.96 (moe-gap-1030.md:14, :62). - At -9 us, d32s is 0.1237*ln(190.5/181.5) = 0.60%, not 1.40%, and d32f is 0.11%. #1 drops to ~5.7%. - The d16, v16 and v32 cuts match the board gaps (9.8, 41.9 and 46.9 us).
- #4 glue, mechanism: the gle_o deferred-normalization fold runs at T=1 only. - GLE_T = {1: True}, commented '# T = 8 keeps 0389's b1' (pub :13238). - gle_pr only accepts M <= 8 (:13528-13530). At T=8 the fold would need 2T = 16 u/v rows. - So v1s and v1f still run a b1 launch, and the v1 share (9 + 8 us) cannot come from the fold.
- #4 glue, size: - their_us 4.0-4.8 us is the marginal saving from skipping all three glue launches (NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:31), not their glue kernel time. Comparing it with our 15.4 us of PDL-inflated kernel durations overstates the gap. - Our node anatomy prices removable T<=32 glue at ~6.4 us per row (h100-dglue-1030.md:28-30) and caps all glue on all rows at x1.049 (:35). - Corrected: ~1.0-1.5%, not 2.6%. Example at 4 us per row: d1s 0.54%, v1s 0.34%, d1f 0.17%, v1f 0.11%.
- #3 and #6 double-count at T<=8: - #3 compares our b12p / dl_pooler_router, which carry router work, against their bf16-board pooler-only times (13.36 / 13.55 us, RESULTS.md). - #6 separately credits 5-7 us of router recovery at d1s/v1s. - In-layer, their T<=8 pooler is the fused FP32-router gle_pr (pub :13655-13673; GLE_FRT at :13239). So ~1-2 us per T<=8 row is counted twice. - The 'pooler+router fused at T<=16' path is 'bfpf' with the BF16 hi+lo router (:12285) only at T=16. At T<=8 the fused kernel is the FP32 GLE one.
- #6 DC0 tax: - The rows (beaed2ad vs dfb318ee) are one run per arm, and dfb318ee also carries the E3 prune and DC-AT7e (CLAIMS.md:110). - Per-row judge swing is 1.5-2.5%, up to ~6% on v1s (FACTS:57), i.e. ±2-9 us. - The size is still corroborated independently by FACTS:93: separate FP32 GEMV +5.5 us at d1 and +6.5 us at v1; F.linear +19.8-20.4 us at v1.
- #2 attention: - From the agent's own in-decode numbers, the sliding gaps are +3.3..+7.1 us (v16s 15.1 vs 11.77), not +4..+7. - The precision shortcuts (rounded-P row sum, lazy max) exist only in the q_len-8 path. They explain none of the d1/d16/d32 gap, and a compliant port loses part of the v* gain.
- Totals: - The seven items sum to ~21% headline (log ~0.21) against a 0.26-0.27 log gap. - After the #1, #4 and #3/#6 corrections the sum is ~18-19%, about 70% of the gap. The rest is spread across rows, and no single mechanism closes it.

### Why the gap persists here (inventory agent)

1) The answer is an assembly of three better components, and decode inherits every component gap. Their decode is vjeux's ATT7 attention (attention board 2.653) + their moe engine (2.4015) + 12-bit sk2 dense GEMVs (bf16 1.5502), joined by PDL cluster glue. d1s takes 69.3 us, about the sum of their component rows (5.2 + 24.6 + 8.8 + 5.2 + 13.4 + 3.6 + 3.7 = 64.5) plus ~5 us of glue. Our own component boards trail theirs: attention 1.903 (x1.39), moe 2.100 (x1.14), bf16 1.343 (x1.15). Decode does not even carry our newest component kernels: d16s still runs the Triton sliding kernel (14.8 us), and BF1 was never ported. The byte cap blocks the ports (S1 alone needs +49.5 KB against ~3.3 KB free: arch-mirror CLAIMS:111). 2) The gap is spread, not concentrated. We are slower on all 12 rows (dfb318ee ours/theirs 1.18-1.39; check 8ee22ddc 1.16-1.39). Log-gap shares: d1s 15%, v32s 14%, v1s 14%, d16s 11%, d32s 11%, v16s 10%, the six full rows 3-6% each. By component (public_unmet_gains): MoE architecture ~6.6% headline, attention ~4.7%, dense GEMVs ~3.4%, T<=8 glue ~2.6%, small-T MoE ~1.7%, FP32 router ~1.6%, T>=128 front ~0.8%. So any single mechanism is worth <=1-2%, against a one-run landing margin of ~1.24% (hypotheses/LESSONS.md:28-34) and judge row swings up to 6% (docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:57). 3) Their values only work inside their structure. VT transfers failed 2/2. T1 (their 4-warp CTA) cost +1.9 us (research/dattn-1030.md:213-216). AT14 PDL index overlap and AT1 atomic merge were refuted. MO2 pass order made v16 slower (x0.955-0.963). KPERM needs an x-permute pass that their gather gets free. The persistent MoE skeleton cost 17-19 us empty on our side (arch-mirror LOG.md:31-32). 4) H100-to-H200 transfer: full-attention gaps are invisible on the H100 (v32f +7 us there vs +59 us on the judge). The d1/v1/v32 MoE gaps are HBM rate (moe-gap: d1 node ratio 1.00 vs judge 1.13). Byte-cut codecs flip (BF2 judge 1.12-1.29x slower). The decode node judge failed (CLAIMS:75), and D-decode could not bind their file. 5) Compliance costs us more than them. The R16 fix took the board from 1.7225 to 1.690 (d1s +6.2 us, v1s +10.9 us) because our DC0b is a separate FP32 GEMV, which their NEG #12 already priced at 2.27%. They fused the router (net ~0.07% with KPERM3). Our stricter attention-precision bar also excludes two of their speed sources (rounded-P row sum, lazy max). 6) Operational losses: 43 scored runs withdrawn, the swap-AB prepare hazard killed scored cb182920, and the day's decode rungs (DC0b, E3, DC-AT7e, T=128 fork) netted 1.671 -> 1.699 board, 1.7165/1.740 in checks. 7) The process rules (never a base, one mechanism per rung, clean-room, kill at the first H100 miss) mean ~89 landings at ~1.2% each would be needed across problems (rank-ladder PLAN.md:86-88). Day 1 landed ~14 of ~45 rungs (31%). Net: the right answer is known, but it is an architecture (persistent fused MoE, fused attention, codec GEMVs, cluster PDL glue). Our base can only reach it piecewise through mechanisms each under the noise bar. On the decode board none of our findings beats a public row, so grafts onto their file (G1-G8) total about +0.5..+2% at best, with low confidence.

### Verifier: inventory misclassifications

- engine-d-dense-raw-bytes (CLAIMS.md:48): not a contradiction. - The public's 12-bit sk2 GEMVs with 2 pre-wait PDL stages (pub :9852, :12281-12285) are exactly CLAIMS:48's reopen condition: 'fewer bytes (bulk12) or a launch hidden behind its predecessor in the PDL chain'. - Relation should be: public meets our reopen condition. - Our own 12-bit BF2 lost on the judge because it is decode-issue-bound (public-ladder FINDINGS.md:13, :67-69), so their win is specific to their implementation.
- pdl-engine-a-k1-t2 (CLAIMS.md:50): unmeasured on their side, not a contradiction. - The public uses PDL at T<=32 (PDL_MAX_T, pub :88) into a single MoE launch whose in-kernel routing prologue is what PDL hides. - They never measured PDL vs no-PDL on that launch. - Our refutation was a crash plus an x0.998-1.000 null on the split K1/K2 engine.
- earlier-engines-launch-forms (CLAIMS.md:51): not comparable. - It is a bundle of different engines. Only the R->K1 PDL part maps to the public, and that part is the same as above. - 'K2 at 1 CTA/SM' vs their 1 CTA/SM WGS compares different engines.
- eat18-eaf-fused-w13-w2 (CLAIMS.md:60): consistent with our refutation, not contradicted. - On the H100 node ours ties their fused TINY (ratio 1.00, moe-gap-1030.md:60), and our fixed cost is lower (6.2 vs 9.1 us, :87-88). - Their judge win at d1 is per-expert HBM rate, not fusion. - Our other one-launch forms lost too (EPF, moe CLAIMS.md:110; arch-mirror moe skeleton KILLED, arch-mirror LOG.md:31-32).
- dchain-pdl-o-gemm (CLAIMS.md:61): not a contradiction. - Their o GEMV (bf16 board m1 5.24, m8 5.52 us) is no faster than our Triton swap-AB incumbent (4.5-5 us in CLAIMS:61; bf16 board o m1 5.32 us). - What their T=1 o adds is the gle_o epilogue fold (pub :13690-13741), a different mechanism covered by CLAIMS:66's reopen.
- w6a-glue-folds-single-cta (CLAIMS.md:66): public meets our reopen, not a contradiction. - Their spread folds satisfy the reopen condition: 'a fold that keeps the work spread over CTAs'. - The rows field is wrong: the fold is T=1 only (GLE_T = {1: True}, pub :13238). v1 keeps b1.
- v4xd-forkguard (CLAIMS.md:94) and t128-hot-cold-fork-union (union-confirm CLAIMS.md:112): consistent / not comparable, not contradicted. - Their side-stream losses involve other branches: pooler + router during attention, and Marlin halves on an older base (NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:19-27). - Their own rule (:27) says a side stream wins if it hides more than ~3 us of serial gap. Our fork does: the judge race measured serial/fork x1.017, ~5 us at v16s.
- sized-out-full-verify-rewrite (CLAIMS.md:96): confirms our sizing, not a contradiction. - Their v16f/v32f attention (99.98 / 182.13 us) sits near the parity targets our sizing used (106.7 / 191.3 us), so the +1.9% headline estimate holds. - The refutation was an EV decision, and the line is superseded by DC-AT7e (CLAIMS.md:110).
- wh-hot-half (CLAIMS.md:99): 'public_lacks' is true only for the hot/cold split. - The exact-N part has a public equivalent: per-job n-tiles 1..8 in the WGS stream-K (moe-gap-1030.md:112-114). - The deeper ring maps to their 4th weight slot, which they tested at ±1 us, null (NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:83-86).

### Public compliance flags

- No rule violation found. Precision: FP32 accumulate; SwiGLU in FP32 with inter stored BF16 (finer than SGLang's two BF16 roundings); y kept FP32; (1+w) in FP32; split-unit partials are FP32 red.global.add.v4.f32 (2263-2268), matching SGLang use_fp32_reduce=True (baseline.py:137-138). Dequant is exact via the 2^-126 / 2^64 exponent trick. The PRMT scale decode runs only when every scale byte is 0x08..0x7E (3104-3111, 3332-3333), else the LUT/MAIN_FALLBACK path. SiLU uses __expf/__fdividef, same as ours (compliance-audit-1030.md:79-80)
- Robustness, not a rule: the grid barrier (2628-2636) and the cross-CTA spins (836, 2011, 2014, 2137) assume all grid = SM-count CTAs (3327) are co-resident WITHOUT a cooperative launch (2878-2879, __launch_bounds__(NTL,1) at 2404). Any graft that changes grid size or per-CTA resources (e.g. GC5) must keep co-residency or it can deadlock
- FP32 atomics (red.global.add.v4.f32 at 2267; atomicAdd on ss at 647/933/1101/1693/1773) give run-to-run nondeterministic summation order. The precision is FP32 and compliant, but outputs are not bitwise reproducible
- The scratch state persists across calls (ss double-buffered by launch generation 2541-2559; counters re-zeroed after the barrier 2638-2647). No data-derived caching, but each call's correctness relies on the previous launch's cleanup
- kernel_fn is wrapped by a ctypes libcuda primary-context bind (3546-3601). It runs on the host and only once per thread (TLS), with no sync; it is not in TASK.md:78's rejected list and the judge accepted it (e1bcad5a). Unusual for a reviewer to see
- Prepare-time risk: 4 load_inline modules compile in prepare (3336-3341), MID from a 142,053-char ALT source (3543-3544). Their FACTS:67 reports cold MoE prepare of 273-275 s, once 311.9 s, against the 300 s limit. A check can time out in prepare
- Held-out-weight cliff: if any local scale byte falls outside 0x08..0x7E, prepare drops WGS for MAIN_FALLBACK (3272-3278). That stays correct but is much slower on the nearby-shape reruns
- Process/provenance, not a judge rule: our own campaign guards forbid sending their file or derivatives to okc check/submit (campaigns/2026-10-30-rank-ladder/PLAN.md:119; .worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror/PLAN.md:113-114). The user's request for separate, non-scoring public-base submissions overrides that; record the override in the new campaign PLAN. Keep header lines 1-6 and add one line naming our modification (Apache-2.0 NOTICE terms; rules/official-rules.txt:257-260). Never send a public-derived file as a scored submit

### Candidates the verifier added

- No missed candidate exists at the moe row level. All six of our judge rows are slower than the public's: muse_moe_w4a16/hypotheses/BEST.result.json reads d1 26.92, d16 99.92, d32 134.17, v1 73.03, v16 213.97, v32 253.13 us; pub RESULTS.md:30-35 reads 24.64 / 92.27 / 123.96 / 63.61 / 175.24 / 207.76 us. The only rows in the 10-30 gap ledger where ours beats theirs are bf16 `o` at m=1 (4.9 vs 5.2 us) and m=8 (5.3 vs 5.5 us). They sit at .worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-30-public-ladder/research/gap-ledger-20261030.txt:25 and :40, mislabeled 'unpooler' (pub RESULTS.md:44 has o m1 at 5.24 us). Both are outside this moe comparison and within noise.
- Phase-A designated finisher: flip OKC_WGSDFIN from 2 to 3. The public compiled it out (pub :1855). Its WGSREORD exists so the phase-A last-stage holder runs early (pub :1946-1953, :2129-2130). Our only support is EPLF's fixed-role form, which routed only at T16 (1.011; campaigns/2026-10-26-expert-bandwidth/LOG.md:2394). One define, on d32/v16/v32. Expected at most 0.5 us per row, which is too small to see, and the public probably tuned it off.
- R1 overturned a public number. Same-address red.v4.f32 costs 1.76-1.80 ns/op on our hardware, not 17 ns (9.7x; ladfacts-1030.md:85). This is the one public fact we overturned that touches the moe kernel's atomics: the ss atomicAdd at pub :647 / :933 and the WGSFINSS 'no shuffles/atomics' choice at pub :219 / :2097. Moving the WGS sum of squares back into the epilogue would drop the final pass's first read pass after the grid barrier (pub :2655-2672). It is weak: WGSFINSS was tuned on the H200 (pub :125-129), the epilogue sits on the unit end that bounds the stream (FACTS:102, epilogue 0.4-0.6 us), and the dropped pass is L2-resident. Expected at most 0.5 us at v32.
- Negative steers the agent did not record. These are our evidence against editing the public file, so the aggressive plan should not spend checks on them: (a) A per-token last-arriver finalize in place of the grid barrier: CLAIMS.md:102 refuted it (the fused tail was 4.7-57 us slower). (b) Dequant running alongside wgmma (ping-pong warpgroups, SS from smem): CLAIMS.md:123 measured x0.60-0.97. The public's GS=2 + wg_wait<1> (pub :2186-2242) already avoids it. (c) Further dequant ALU cuts: CLAIMS.md:117 says 7 ALU + 2 FMA per word is the minimum, which is exactly pub DQ7 (:161-164). (d) Deeper rings: CLAIMS.md:92 and pub NEG:83-86 agree. (e) A barrier-free run-end exchange: CLAIMS.md:120 found the wait only moves. This applies to the public's per-unit CTA_SYNC at pub :898 and helper0:1474.

### Verifier corrections to the unmet-gains list

- U1 (v16 WGS dequant-once), why_not_transferred: it blames the x feed ('cp.async pieces with per-piece barriers', the xf/xe handshake). Our newest evidence says the feed is not the loss. MO3-FEED (CLAIMS.md:134) found every wide variant does 2 stages of work for 1.1-1.7 stages of cost, 'so the loss lies outside the stage (stream-K cost weighting, 4-tile partials, per-pass x-barrier, K1 gathered rows; not isolated)'. campaigns/2026-10-30-rank-ladder/LANES.tsv:2 says 'stage feed is not the loss'. CLAIMS:133's 'likely cause x feed' is the older guess and was never isolated. The our_attempts field quotes ':134 (only WT clears 1.1x)' but drops that conclusion.
- U1 sizing: '~20-30 of 38.7 us (moe-gap-1030.md:94)' was sized against the older 41.9 us gap (moe-gap:14, ours 217.1 us), using a +101 MB byte count. CLAIMS.md:130 (MO2, later) found the repeat passes were already L2-served, so the excess is re-dequant per pass, not bytes. The byte sizing is superseded, and the -21 us MO3 S1 projection is consume-only and per-SM (CLAIMS.md:132). Its in-situ form lost 78-80 us (CLAIMS.md:133). Note also that moe-gap-1030.md:80-81 ('NOT L2 hits') conflicts with CLAIMS.md:130.
- U2 (v32 single kernel): - 'Closing it all = +3.4%' should be ln(253.13/207.76)/6 = 3.29%, i.e. +3.3%. moe-gap's +3.5% used 254.7 us. - 'align ~7.5' is stale: ms_align replaced _hm_align in mstack (CLAIMS.md:119, c5e2d8d4). - 'arch-mirror skeleton campaign/arch-mirror CLAIMS:135' is listed as a v32 attempt, but that line's rows are d1/d16/d32 only. The skeleton was never measured at T=256.
- U4 (d1 TINY): - 'TINY consumer at the per-unit floor (1.12 us per 36 KB unit, FACTS:107)' misattributes the number. pub docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:107 is the MID path at d16; the public publishes no per-unit time for TINY. So 'theirs sits at it' is unsupported. - '~15 instr/word est. from 836-889' is a hand estimate with no SASS count. - 'The H100 cannot show the gap: node d1 ratio 1.00 (moe-gap) vs 1.156 (LANES.tsv:6)' contradicts itself: 1.156 is a node reading that does show the gap. The two node runs disagree, so the d1 transfer is unresolved, not impossible.
- U5 (d32): our_attempts says 'v4x/EPLF CLAIMS:84/:95/:97 landed'. EPLF routed only at T16 (CLAIMS.md:97 'routed at T16'; LOG.md:2394), so it never ran on d32, which runs v4cxs (CLAIMS.md:84). Also :95 is tagged [shelf], not landed.
- U6 (d16 MID): this is the public's own d16 limiter, not something it gains. FACTS:107 says phase B is consumer-bound (issue 51.9%, 0.82 eligible warps), and split experts read their weights twice (7.7-11% of bytes; helper0 line 364: 'a split expert re-streams its weights'). Also, the MID kernel body is the 2,691-line ALT source (pub :3543-3544); public_location 145-149 covers only the defines.
- U7 (L2 hints): the public does not use createpolicy. It passes immediate 64-bit policy constants (pub :299-307, 0x12F0... and 0x14F0...), a different form from the 'createpolicy / .L2::cache_hint helper family' our crash-715 note names (muse_moe_w4a16/submission.py:927-928). It also does not transfer to our engine: evict-first on weights would evict lines our multi-pass experts reuse (CLAIMS.md:130: repeat passes are L2-served).
- U8 (KPERM3): 'with the x permute free in the gather' is wrong. Every CTA's consumer threads run an explicit gather pass before the weight producers start (pub :2026-2073, :2586-2590). It sits on the serial prologue (FACTS:99, ramp 3-6 us); the permute is free only because that gather already exists. The '~+1.6% headline' adds up per-row deltas; the measured same-hour board gain is +1.25% (FACTS:115).
- U3 (v1 TINY2) checks out (pub :654-656, :700-715, :856-877: one SCDK scale pair per 64-K chunk, reused across 4 k16 steps; x loaded per lane once per job). One clarification: per FACTS:115, d1/d16/v1 do not run the WGS/KPERM3 build. Their scale sharing comes from this skinny layout, not from KPERM3.

### Why the gap persists here (inventory agent)

1) **The gap is spread over every row, and the rungs were smaller than the noise.** Ours/theirs is 1.09 / 1.08 / 1.08 / 1.15 / 1.22 / 1.22 (d1..v32). The log-gap shares are v16 25.5%, v32 25.2%, v1 17.6%, d1 11.3%, d16 10.2%, d32 10.1%. We have never beaten them on any row (best-ever d32 132.13 vs 123.96), so there is no row-level mechanism of ours to graft back. Closing x1.144 means moving every row. Our one-mechanism rungs were 0.2-1% each:
- XL trims ~0.2% (CLAIMS:114-115).
- MO1' +0.95%, under margin (CLAIMS:127).
- C-moe null on both node and judge (CLAIMS:129).
These sit inside the judge's ±1.5% CI and the per-row identity spreads of 1.3-6.2 us (REPLICATION.md:20).

2) **Their gains come from coupled structure, and each transplant arrived without its neighbours.** The public is:
- one persistent launch per call: routing in every CTA, phases A/B linked by per-job doneA and per-(job,stage) doneAs counters, one grid barrier, a distributed final pass (2404-2786);
- 1 CTA/SM with warp specialisation, which leaves room for x in registers (699-721), 3 weight slots plus 3 x slots of 32 KB (1819-1824), and 4 WGs sharing one x tile;
- x gathered once per call into a stage-major KPERM3 layout (2024-2073), which makes KPERM free and lets wide-N jobs take x as one bulk copy per stage.

Each of our transplants hit a missing neighbour:
- MO1 needed a new x-permute pass, est. 1.5-3 us (CLAIMS:126).
- MO3 S2 fed x by cp.async pieces inside a 2-CTA/SM smem budget (~107/113 KB): x0.79, +78-80 us (CLAIMS:133). The isolated stage bench showed the stage itself costs only 1.1-1.7x (CLAIMS:134), so the loss came from the surrounding structure.
- X2 (x in registers) gave x1.064 but forfeits co-residency at our 96-112 register cap (CLAIMS:75).
- Combine fusion by the last-arriving CTA serialised, 4-56 us slower (CLAIMS:102). They use a grid barrier plus a segmented pass instead.

3) **Our clean-room skeleton was not their skeleton.** Arch-mirror L3 used a cooperative launch (~5 us), two grid barriers (~2.5 us each), 264 CTAs and a one-CTA-per-token combine (10 us at T=1). Its empty skeleton alone cost 17-19 us (am-moe-s0.md:56-60). The public's whole node-fitted fixed cost at d1 is 9.1 us including real work (moe-gap-1030.md:88). EPF (fixed 11.1 vs 4.1 us, CLAIMS:110) and UFS (readiness waits +6..12 us, CLAIMS:124) also lost on costs the public does not pay. The one-launch direction was refuted three times, each time on a form the public does not use. The public lines are 2878-2879 (plain launch), 2628-2636 (one barrier) and 2710-2755 (segmented pass).

4) **Our own structure is locally optimal and resists their pieces.**
- 2 CTAs/SM beats 1 CTA/SM for our consumer (CLAIMS:99: 0.943/0.897).
- The hot/cold fork is worth x1.03-1.07 (CLAIMS:88-89).
- Per-class prepare races choose the routes.

Every public piece needs their 1-CTA/SM resource shape, which our engine gives up. Values moved without their structure failed 2/2 (arch-mirror PLAN context). Their own WGS32 tile failed on their base for schedule reasons (NEG:98-101).

5) **The node misreads the levers that matter on the judge.**
- moe is HBM-walled on the H100. E4/E5 read ~1.00 on the node but +3-5% on the judge (CLAIMS:106-107).
- Riders cannot be resolved on the node (public-ladder FINDINGS.md:15).
- Node routing draws flip races: 0.913 node vs 0.963 judge (CLAIMS:112); X0b (CLAIMS:65).
- The d1 gap is invisible on one node run (ratio 1.00) and visible on another (1.156) (moe-gap-1030.md:60; rank-ladder LANES.tsv:6).

Gating on the node filtered out exactly the per-expert-rate and fixed-cost levers.

6) **Where we are ahead is too small to matter.** On the node, our d1 fixed cost is 6.2 vs 9.1 us, and v16 costs us 8.69 vs 9.15 us per distinct expert with a 3.3 vs 19.3 us intercept. These are swamped by their per-route 0.015 vs our 0.44 us at v16 (re-dequant per 16-route pass) and their per-expert 9.6 vs our 11.0 us at d1 (moe-gap-1030.md:75-88).

7) **Compliance and bytes boxed us in.**
- The BF16 atomic split-K had to go (CLAIMS:128). Their FP32 red.add costs them nothing extra.
- Our file is 982,138 B with 17.9 KB free because it carries v1-v6, v4x, E4/E5/XL, wh5ds, the Marlin/Triton fallbacks and the races. MO3 S2 alone needed +12 KB.
- They cover all six rows in 326 KB.

A structural rewrite on our base has to replace engines, not add arms. That is why a public-base check track (673 KB headroom) is the faster way to test whether anything we learned adds value. The graft candidates there are all sync, fix-up or epilogue tweaks (GC1-GC10, ~0.1-1% each, mostly speculative): our findings mostly agree with or were already absorbed by the public design (L3/L4, DQ7 = A4b, cost-weighted stream-K, no 64-bit divides, ring depth 3, tail rebalancing null, no ping-pong).

### Verifier: inventory misclassifications

- k-slice-consumer: 'public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation' is wrong. Our refutation (CLAIMS.md:51) is narrow: B-fragment loads are not Engine A's limiter. The public splits K across warps (pub :654-656) so that x fits in registers (64 per thread at 1 CTA/SM), and it never isolates K-split as a gain. The matching line of ours is CLAIMS.md:75 (X2, x in registers, x1.064 at 1 CTA/SM), where the public AGREES with our positive result.
- w6m-fork: 'public_negative_contradicts' is wrong. The public's negatives are about decode-layer side streams: the router/pooler before attention, Marlin halves on an earlier base, and a graph-START fork's second root branch (NEG:20-27; FACTS:52). The public never measured a MoE hot/cold fork, while ours is judge-measured (CLAIMS.md:55, v32 x1.023; also :88-89). Correct relation: public_lacks (different scope).
- epf: 'public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation' overstates it. On the node the public's fused TINY ties our chain (d1 ratio 1.00 [0.999, 1.043], moe-gap-1030.md:60). Its fit shows MORE fixed cost than our chain (9.1 vs 6.2 us, moe-gap:88). That agrees with EPF's measured mechanism, fusion adding fixed cost (11.1 vs 4.1 us, CLAIMS.md:110). The public's judge lead at d1 is per-expert H200 rate the node cannot show (moe-gap:60, :99). Correct relation: public_consistent; fusion is not what wins d1.
- v4xr-piece-first: 'public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation' rests on WGSREORD (pub :1852, :1946-1953). The public published no isolated gain for it (no REORD entry in docs/NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md or H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md), and it serves a different goal: making the phase-A finisher run early (:2129-2130). Correct relation: public_has_analog_unmeasured.
- arch-mirror-l3-skeleton: not a contradiction. The public avoids exactly the pieces our gate costed. Instead of a cooperative launch (~5 us), two barriers and a one-CTA combine (10 us; am-moe-s0.md:56-60), it uses a plain launch (pub :2878-2879), one barrier (:2628-2636), and a combine split into 4 column segments per token (FINCOL, :2710-2755). This is consistent with our cost breakdown, and only partly meets CLAIMS:135's reopen condition (one barrier, not the 'no-grid-barrier variant' it names). Correct relation: public_differs_in_costed_pieces.
- engine-S: the label is defensible but imprecise. CLAIMS.md:60's reopen condition is '3-4 consumer WGs lift the dequant rate', and the public WGS runs 4 consumer warpgroups (pub :130 NW=16, :271, :1812-1815). Our refutation was itself a positive below the bar (judge x1.02 faster than Engine A). Better stated: public_meets_our_reopen_condition (agrees in sign).
