### Public file facts

PUB = /home/user/git/okc-muse-spark-kernels/kernels/muse_bf16_proj.py, alygg77@6cae49a.

Size and identity:
- 382,614 B, 6,434 lines. sha256 290a86ab852c851ad0307002a55a432a1ffce44d7ef51d529e19284193647b90, the same as results.json kernel_sha256, so the clone is the judged file.
- Judge job f07235a6ca8a46e2: 1.5502x [1.5269, 1.5734], score_us 3116.3 vs our 3598.25. Rank 75/1401 on 10-29; rank-50 threshold 1.5604.
- The public team's own unchanged-file spread is 1.5259-1.5502 (H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:61).

Header:
- Line 1 is '# okc: problem=muse_bf16_proj'.
- Lines 2-4: Matsvei attribution. Lines 5-28: Nathen Fernandes MIT notice. Line 29: vjeux Apache. Line 30: alygg77.
- As a check submission the file is complete as-is. A graft keeps the header and edits below it.

Structure:
- 12+ layered prepare/kernel_fn wrappers. The outermost wins.
- Live route per row, outermost first:
  - pooler m16: Nathen cps (6427).
  - pooler m8: Nathen smallm2 (5733).
  - o m8: v18s (5100).
  - router m1: rgemv (4662).
  - o m128/m256: olayer/rl (4568).
  - m=1 qkv/unpooler: raced cgemv variant (4462).
  - qkv m128: qbig (4337).
  - router m2..16: rsmall (4277).
  - klarge cuBLASLt pins for qkv256, pooler128, pooler256, o256 (shadowed by rl), unpooler256 (4036).
  - Everything else: the pinned _autotune choice (4116-4125): cgemv at m=1, sk2 at m<=32 (with escape-ext at qkv/unpooler m2..8 and the u32 geometry for unpooler m32), torchT for unpooler m128, rk for router m128/256.
- CUTLASS and the gq/gqx/wide/TMA-skinny routes are dead at the scored shapes.

Compile:
- No nvcc at import. Every load_inline runs in prepare: main TU _get_ext (~25 s per PUB:2800 comment) plus about 12 more extensions (escape, u32, small router, qbig, iv_cg, iv_r24, olayer, rl, v18s, smallm2, cps), and CUTLASS in a background thread with a 170 s join cap.
- Public compile time on the judge is not documented. Our own file imports in 220-290 s on the H100 (bf1-1030.md:85; bfvt-1030.md:50). Prepare cap is 300 s and the whole run cap is 10 min, so every graft that adds an extension needs headroom (see G9).

Tuned tables:
- SK2_CONFIGS/SK2_PICK 3073-3106, SK2_BALANCE 3257-3258, CG_CONFIGS 3204, RK_SPLITS 3680.
- _KLARGE_CONFIGS 4008-4015, _PINNED_LARGE 4110-4115, _QBIG_CONFIG 4295, _OC_OL/_OC_RL 4555-4556, _U_CFG 4622.
- _VO_CONFIG 5078, _NP_SMALLM2_ROUTES 5359/5399, _NDD tables 5804/5951-5955, _IV_* 4357/4378.
- Several CUDA sources are zlib+base64 payloads (qbig, olayer/rl, router, v18s, smallm2, smtrace, cps); editing one means decoding and re-encoding.

Precision and budgets:
- FP32 accumulation everywhere; uses cuBLASLt from libcublasLt.so.13 (CUDA 13 judge image).
- Byte budget is not binding: the okc refusal is at 1,000,000 B (arch-mirror PLAN.md:123), leaving ~617 KB of headroom. Our master is 408,074 B (blob f6cf9245).

Running it as a check:
- The file needs nothing beyond itself plus the user's explicit license/provenance decision.
- okc returns the earlier result for byte-identical code within a mode (hypotheses/LESSONS.md:20-22). Whether that holds across accounts is unknown, so an unchanged-public control might need a trivial byte change.
- Use a same-day unchanged-public control and per-row reads. Single-check headline CI is ±1.5%, while every graft above is predicted at <= +0.2% headline.

What I could not verify: public per-kernel times (no public profile; no D-bf16 side-by-side ever run) and the public compile time.

### Public compliance flags

- No blocking violation found. Precision: all BF16 weights are lossless 12-bit codes with exact FP32 escape deltas asserted in prepare (PUB:41-53, 252-256), plus BF16 twin fallbacks (PUB:5004-5006, 5287-5289). The router runs hi+lo BF16 pairs (rsmall, sk2 HL, rk) or FP32 (rgemv) with FP32 accumulation, which the rules allow at any batch (TASK.md Correctness/Rules). All partials are FP32.
- rk router (m=128/256) adds partials with red.relaxed.gpu.global.add.v4.f32, so results differ run to run (~1e-7 relative) (PUB:2580-2583, 2712). The precision is not lower, but a reviewer may notice.
- rk clears the output inside the kernel using a monotonic device counter kept in state across calls (state['rk_zc'] PUB:3436; PUB:2584-2586, 2636-2645) and traps after 2 s if not every CTA is co-resident. That is device state mutated by the timed call; it is not output caching, but it is a robustness risk.
- Pinned routes key on the exact m (qbig m==128 PUB:4339; olayer/rl m==128/256 PUB:4572-4586; Nathen pooler m==8 PUB:5737 and m==16 PUB:6431; v18s o m==8 PUB:5102; klarge exact keys PUB:4040). Nearby m falls back to older routes or torch (PUB:3814-3834). This is exposure in the nearby-shape review, not a violation.
- A CUTLASS build runs in a background thread with an nvcc subprocess during prepare (PUB:3397-3402, 2891-2911). Raw ctypes calls go to libcuda.so.1 (cuCtxSetCurrent / cuDevicePrimaryCtxRetain, PUB:3865-3885) and libcublasLt.so.13 (PUB:3897-3997). None of this is on the rules' rejected list (no harness frames, no module edits, no network), and the judge accepted it, but the 'starts processes' wording deserves a note.
- PUB:113 reads the SK2_DELAY_ESC environment variable (a debug-only stall). It is harmless unless set; a static checker could still flag environment reads.
- Dead but present: iv_router24 scales the router's lo half by 2^shift and 12-bit-compresses hi/lo, with a losslessness check (PUB:4427-4443). It is shadowed at m=1 by rgemv (PUB:4660-4665). NU smtrace, build_router/prepare_router and tt_mw m32w are defined but never wired.
- Provenance and license: the file carries Nathen Fernandes' MIT notice (PUB:5-28) and Apache attributions for vjeux, alygg77 and Matsvei (PUB:2-4, 29-30). Sending it, or a graft of it, even as an off-board check is a submission under the challenge terms ('you confirm you have the right to', rules/technical-rules.llms.txt Terms). It also overrides this repo's clean-room rule and the open bf16 MIT question (.worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror/PLAN.md:36-40,125-126,185). The user should decide this explicitly before any check is sent.

At pooler m16 this reads ~64 MB of x from L2 against 50 MB of weights. | pooler d16 13.54 -> 13.0-13.5 (0..-0.5 us) x 0.356%/us = 0..+0.18%. With WR=1, the 8 warps of a CTA read disjoint K ranges (decoded cps template lines 201-207, 227, 249-260), so a CTA has no intra-CTA x duplication for a shared TMA box to remove. The ~64 MB of L2 x reads are cross-CTA (256 CTAs each reading all of x), which only multicast could share. Our multicast evidence says sharing x across CTAs adds nothing: CLAIMS.md:74 (judge mc/uc 0.995-1.007) and :75 (H100 0.97-1.02). Our BF1 x-box win (bf1-1030.md:80-83) fixed per-row cp.async.bulk copies serialised in one producer lane (CLAIMS.md:50: 30-50 ns each). cps has no producer lane; it issues 16-B ld.global.nc per lane from every warp in parallel. |

### Candidates the verifier added

- No row exists where our judge time beats theirs (muse_bf16_proj/hypotheses/BEST.result.json vs /home/user/git/okc-muse-spark-kernels/RESULTS.md:41-72, after SGLang-baseline normalisation). The three ties are unpooler v16 19.09/19.07 (our nvjet NN layout, CLAIMS.md:44, vs their torchT, PUB:4113), router d1 3.71/3.69 (our BF4 FP32 register GEMV, CLAIMS.md:82, vs rgemv, PUB:4662-4665) and o v32 9.28/9.26 (our nvjet 128x128 vs their custom rl, PUB:4580-4586). Each tie is between equivalent mechanisms, so neither side has a graft. The only notable point is that the public's custom rl kernel gains nothing over plain cuBLAS at o m256.
- WEAK, likely null: the public m2..8 escape walk (PUB:4061-4069, used for qkv and unpooler-slice at m=2..8 by the ESC ext, PUB:4049-4107) is the same serial pattern that our b12 work measured as a pathology. Each iteration loads ptab_s[pe] from smem, then one global x value per lane (`x[(size_t)lane*K+kk]`), then does a read-modify-write of cr[] in smem. corr and ptab_s are un-restricted shared pointers (PUB:1029, 1032), so the compiler cannot hoist the next load past the store, which serialises one global latency per escape. Ours: CLAIMS.md:75-76; done/gain/2026-10-29T200122Z-b12-batched-escape-m8-32-race.md:129 (~0.43 us per serial load); judge GA0-GA1 2.43 us (qkv m8) and 3.84 us (pooler m8) at archive/b12-batched-escape-m8-32-race/bced57b42b87444d.log:85-86. Graft: batch-load all of a warp's escape x values into registers, then accumulate in the same order (bitwise identical). Caveat: in the public the walk runs concurrently with the stream (escape warps decoupled from the ring, PUB:4057), and only qkv m8 (~26 escapes/CTA / 4 warps = ~6.5 serial loads, about 2.8 us against a ~6 us stream) is long enough to matter. It probably stays hidden. Predicted 0..+0.1% headline (qkv v1, unpooler v1). Run a per-CTA globaltimer stamp diag before building anything.
- WEAK: rsmall on a 128-CTA x cluster-8 grid. Nathen's ROUTER_SMALL already compiles RSM(8,8) (decoded payload line 84), but state packs only (16,8) (PUB:4751), and alygg77's rsmall is hard-wired to S=16 (PUB:4226-4227). Our CLAIMS.md:81 (BF4c-1) measured the 256 x cluster16 grid 1.019-1.066x SLOWER than 128 x cluster8 at m2..8 on the H100 with the same load path. Rows: router v1/d16. Predicted -0.1..+0.1 us per row (<= 0.05% each). This is the 'latency-side tweak on a small kernel' class that public-ladder FINDINGS.md:12 says does not transfer, and the public picked s16w8 on the H200 (PUB:4239 extension name), so this is below the bar.

### Verifier corrections to the unmet-gains list

- All five entries: est_headline_share uses SGLang-baseline-normalised times, but our_us lists raw judge times. Example: pooler d16 raw gap 17.97-13.54 = 4.43 us (1.25%), while the 3.94 us / 58.54 in the text is the normalised gap. The method is never stated. Where the two SGLang runs differ by >4%, the share is uncertain by roughly +/-0.2%.
- Unmet #4 (m=1), o d1: the raw gap is 0.08 us (5.32 vs 5.24). The 0.23% share comes entirely from baseline normalisation (our SGLang 6.50 us vs their 7.04, an 8% baseline swing). Treat o d1 as a tie, which makes the m=1 total ~1.5%, not 1.74%. Likewise qkv d1 is 0.73% raw vs 0.56% normalised, and pooler v16 (unmet #2) is 0.36% raw vs 0.59% normalised (baseline 24.51 vs 25.65).
- Unmet #1: 'escape-balanced rows' applies to qkv_gate only. SK2_BALANCE_LAYERS = {'qkv_gate'} (PUB:3258): 'o/unpooler m=32 lost'.
- Unmet #1: the qkv d32 public time (10.78) sits on the public's measured CTA-stage saturation floor, which is independent of ring depth, x loads and the escape warp (docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:81; NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:53-57). That 0.43% is the codec's byte saving only; no launch-value change can reach it.
- Unmet #1: the unpooler m32 live config is the u32_r96 override (R=32, NT=2, NWR=2, WSK=2, UPW=2; PUB:4622, 4649-4653), not unpooler32_r144 (PUB:3081). The cited 4622-4657 is right, but the SK2_PICK entry (PUB:3105) is dead for m32.
- Unmet #2: the klarge (o,256) tn pin (PUB:4013) is dead. The olayer/rl wrapper is defined after klarge (kernel_fn at PUB:4568 vs 4036) and intercepts o m=256 with the custom rl kernel (PUB:4580-4586). That rl kernel ties our nvjet (9.26 vs 9.28), so the o v32 0.03% is noise.
- Unmet #2: 'rank-ladder LANES.tsv has D-moe/D-attn only' is incomplete. campaigns/2026-10-30-rank-ladder/LANES.tsv:7 also has decode-D (blocked: 'shims cannot bind the public file API'). The core point still holds: there is no bf16 lane.
- Unmet #2: the qkv m128 '17.38 -> 15.55' Lt evidence is in DIAGNOSIS.md:58/106, not CLAIMS.md:65. The ~0.5%-estimated m256 sweep shelf is CLAIMS.md:67 (DECISIONS:412).
- Unmet #3: wrong line refs. qkv8t_r144 is PUB:3086 (config) and 3097 (pick). slice8_r192 is PUB:3080 and 3103 (not 3084/3092/3100). Mechanism wording: at m2..8 the distinguishing change is not 'per-warp escape scratch'. The 4 escape warps are taken off the ring (empty-barrier count cut to NW, PUB:4057), read x straight from global, and stride the escape table (PUB:4061-4069). It is concurrent, not batched.
- Unmet #5: router SK2_PICK lines are PUB:3100-3102 (not 3101-3103), and rr_small dispatch is PUB:4226-4228. The total is 1.19% without the d1 tie and 1.22% with it. Also missing: an m<=32 MT=4 rsmall already exists in Nathen's ROUTER_SMALL (decoded line 83), and neither author routes it, so 'sk2 HL at m=32' was a deliberate choice.
- Unmet #1 why_not_transferred: our BF2 component costs include a '12-bit load shape +1.0 us' (bfvt-1030.md:117-118). The public per-lane format is exactly two LDS per n8-tile per unit (ld.shared.v4 low bytes + ld.shared.v2 codes, 768 B per unit; PUB:1179-1182, TU=768 at PUB:3116). The format difference is part of the mechanism and should be listed next to off-path escapes and decode8.

### Why the gap persists here (inventory agent)

Size and shape of the gap (BEST.result.json job 7753d5c7 vs RESULTS.md:37-72, each row normalized by its own run's SGLang time):
- Headline gap x1.1546 = 1.5502/1.3425. Log shares by load: d16 3.72%, v1 2.82%, d32 2.75%, d1 1.88%, v32 1.68%, v16 1.52%.
- By mechanism family: 12-bit m16/32 stream 5.2% (+ o 0.3%), m=128/256 GEMMs 2.45%, m=8 routes 2.3%, m=1 GEMV 1.74%, router 1.22%.
- On 0 of 30 rows do we beat them. Best rows are ties: unpooler v16 19.06 vs 19.07, router d1 3.77 vs 3.69, o v32 9.43 vs 9.26. So the strongest graft class (a row where ours wins) is empty, and every graft candidate is speculative at <= ~0.2% headline each.

Why one-mechanism rungs did not close it:

1. The codec win is a co-design, and we transplanted only half of it.
   - BF1 copied their data path with BF16 bytes. It landed but sits at 3.7 TB/s of BF16, which cannot go below the 14.0 us SoL at pooler m16.
   - BF2 put our 12-bit code inside it. Our consumer does the escape walk and the heavier 12-bit load/decode on the per-stage critical path: ~2.8 us, SM-clock-bound (bfvt-1030.md:115-123). That cost does not shrink with the H200's HBM rate, so the H100 win (0.82-0.88) flipped on the judge (1.117-1.217; CLAIMS:78,86).
   - The public's sk2 keeps the consumer cheap: decode8 at ~10 int ops per 8 weights (PUB:212-219), escape corrections done by dedicated warps from the ring's x tiles (PUB:1110-1158), and NACC plus balanced rows. Nathen's cps has no producer handoff at all.
   - BF2b/BF2c/BF2R each tuned OUR consumer (more warps, early release) and failed (CLAIMS:84,90). VT moved only launch values (CLAIMS:88).
   - These are values and mechanisms that only work inside their structure.

2. Misattribution.
   - The ladder took its targets from the public docstring and the rows, not from the live route chain.
   - BF5 matched the docstring's CUTLASS ratio (0.907 = CLAIMS:79), but the live pooler m256 route is a pinned cuBLASLt rownn plan that shadows CUTLASS (PUB:4008-4044). That leaves 3 us at 24.36 vs our 27.34.
   - The BF1 target 'pooler m16 13.5' (LADDER.md:28) is Nathen's MIT cps route, not sk2.
   - A grep of the whole musespark repo finds zero mentions of klarge, qbig, cgemv, decodediet, smallm2, olayer or rownn.
   - Unlike attention, where measuring their kernels next to ours produced the AT8F landing (arch-mirror PLAN.md:33), bf16 never had a D-lane (rank-ladder LANES.tsv: D-moe and D-attn only).

3. Transfer.
   - H100 screens for memory-streaming codecs and small-kernel latency tweaks do not transfer (public-ladder FINDINGS.md:9-17).
   - BF2: H100 0.82-0.88, judge 1.12-1.29. BF4c-2: H100 0.92-0.95, judge 1.017.
   - So the screen forwarded rungs that lost on the judge and could not reveal the H200-side gap.

4. Bundles decomposed into parts that do not pay alone.
   - rsmall = cluster-16 grid + register-direct hi/lo fragments + push.
   - We tested the parts separately: BF4c-1 lost 1.019-1.066, BF4c-2 did not route (1.017), and only push routed (0.866).
   - The H100 stack (0.806-0.816) was never raced on the judge, which leaves router v1 at 4.88 vs 4.38.

5. Direction errors at m=1 and m=8.
   - Our row-owner family moved to fewer, bigger CTAs (R=32, 1 CTA/SM refuted, CLAIMS:60).
   - The public uses thousands of 2-row CTAs with everything in flight (cgemv).
   - LESSONS.md:196 ('the codec does not beat the cluster split-K at the m=1 unpooler slice') is contradicted by their 3.58 us vs our 4.71.

6. Policy constraints.
   - The m8/m16 pooler and m8 o rows the public wins are Nathen's MIT routes.
   - arch-mirror parked bf16 for exactly that reason (PLAN.md:125-126,185), and /hypothesize minted 0 bf16 leads ('bf16 gap = the parked MIT pooler', LOG.md:48-49).
   - Those rows alone carry pooler d16 1.12% + pooler v1 0.86% + o v1 0.35% of the headline gap.

7. Noise versus increment size.
   - Per-row judge noise is 1-5% (CLAIMS:63) and a single check's CI is ±1.5%.
   - Per-us headline sensitivity is only 0.20-0.48% (1/(6 x load time)).
   - Each 1-3 us mechanism is worth 0.2-0.6%, below one check's margin, so a rung can rarely prove itself alone.
   - The public reached 1.55 by stacking ~11 wrapper layers, and saw a 1.6% spread on one unchanged file itself.

8. Not the cause: the byte budget (408 KB of 1 MB) and compliance cost (our file was audited COMPLIANT, compliance-audit-1030.md:15-16,109). Compile/prepare time is a latent constraint for any stack (220-290 s import on the H100).

What this implies for the aggressive check track (non-scoring, public base):
- Run an unchanged-public control on the same day.
- Run reverse-ablation arms: the public file with ONE live route swapped for ours (BF1 at pooler d16, BF5 CUTLASS at pooler v32, hilo+PDL at router v16, b12m at pooler v1). Within one run these price each public mechanism against ours on the judge.
- Run the graft arms G1-G10, each behind an in-prepare read-flush race with the public route as fallback, read per row.
- Expect the grafts to add at most ~+0.5-0.9% headline combined. Most of the 15.5% lives in mechanisms the public already has.

Unverified: public per-kernel times, public compile time, and whether the public tried cps at unpooler m16.

### Verifier: inventory misclassifications

- lt-algo-pick-writeflush (CLAIMS.md:47): should be public_positive_consistent_with_our_reopen, not 'contradicts our refutation'. Our refutation was about the timing method (write flush), and its reopen clause is 'rank per cell under the read flush', which CLAIMS.md:65 already landed. The public's pins are offline H200 sweep constants (docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:82). The public_evidence also lists 'o m256 tn' as a live pin, but it is shadowed by rl (PUB:4580-4586).
- router-ticket-no-memset (CLAIMS.md:49): should be public_lacks (different mechanism) and the public's own data agrees with our refutation. docs/H200_MEASURED_FACTS.md:141: 'A merge by the last-arriving CTA through global partials plus __threadfence is 2 to 4 us slower'. rk never runs a finalize; it uses red.add with an in-kernel clear overlapped with the mainloop (PUB:2579-2586, 2630-2650, 2712). That matches our FINDINGS lesson that 'a finalize run by one CTA is a trap' (public-ladder FINDINGS.md:28).
- khalf-fp32-partials-pooler128 (CLAIMS.md:59): should be public_supersedes (a better non-split pinned plan), not 'public_negative_contradicts'. The cited NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:127-130 is about qkv m256 split-K-7 under the per-SM L2 write limit, not pooler m128 K-halves. The relevant public evidence (NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:45-47; FACTS:82, 'no split-K in the top 60') compares split vs pinned non-split plans, while ours compared against the heuristic plan. The two are compatible.
- bf4c1-regrid-cluster16 (CLAIMS.md:81): not a contradiction. Our measurement isolated the grid change (slower alone), and the public uses it only bundled. It should be public_bundles_not_isolated. The public file also carries an S=8 instantiation (Nathen ROUTER_SMALL RSM(8,8)), so the grid choice was available to them either way.
- bf4c2-register-hilo-push (CLAIMS.md:83): only partly right. The judge refuted fk alone (1.017, no route), and the stack was never raced. The public's rsmall contradicts our transfer conclusion ('H100 router gains do not transfer'), not our measurement. It should be public_positive_on_an_untested_stack.
- bf4d-ouns-push (CLAIMS.md:85): 'public_lacks' is wrong for the mechanism. st.async push reduce is in public rsmall (PUB:4141-4142, 4200), sk2 CL>1 (PUB:1252-1282), qbig (decoded line 17) and cps (decoded line 90). The public lacks only the ouns kernel and uses faster non-cluster routes on these rows. Line refs: slice8_r192 is PUB:3080/3103, not 3085/3100.
- u12s-unpooler-slice-m1 (CLAIMS.md:61): the classification stands, but the cause should be recorded. Our lesson (hypotheses/LESSONS.md:196, 'per-CTA ramp binds') came from a 64-CTA row-owner shape. The public cgemv runs 512 two-row CTAs with all loads up front (PUB:2458, 3204), so our lesson was a shape artifact, not a codec limit.

## muse_prefill_layer

45 mechanisms. Relations: public_does_same 11, public_does_differently 10, public_negative_agrees 8, public_lacks 8, public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation 5, not_comparable 3

### Public file facts

File and size: /home/user/git/okc-muse-spark-kernels/kernels/muse_prefill_layer.py is 292,650 B and 5,383 lines (sha256 ced91e7e...dd20, judge job 7f87198d13a34650, repo 6cae49a; results.json prefill entry). For comparison, ours is 467,607 B (blob 7569df17, board 1.5721, job 9f934846). Both are far below the 1,000,000 B okc refusal.

Header and license: line 1 is '# okc: problem=muse_prefill_layer'. Lines 2-3 carry the vjeux Apache-2.0 attribution (okc-2026 f35a41e) and alygg77's notice (NOTICE:1-28). The file is vjeux's, unchanged.

Imports (:74-84): torch, triton, torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline and sgl_kernel FA3 (try/except).

Build: nothing compiles at import. prepare() calls _build() (:382, :1263-1273): one load_inline of _CUDA_SRC + GLUE (CUDA :1628-5300, glue :1425-1626) with -O3, -gencode compute_90a/sm_90a, -std=c++20 and --use_fast_math. The module name is a sha1 of the source. Templates: 2 moe_fused_persistent, 2 moe_gemm, 2 moe_gemm_persistent, 7 attn_fwd, 2 attn_fwd_sk, plus prep, combine and boundary kernels (:1598-1622). A second, host-only cuBLASLt extension is built by _build_lt (:1276-1281).

Prepare work, in order:
- Repack the NVFP4 banks per expert into a bit-permuted dequant layout (:540-592). MOE_FAST_SCALE is picked from the weights' scale bytes (:360-365).
- _tune_lt: 3 token counts x 5 GEMMs, up to 32 algorithms each, unless pinned (:448-507).
- Validate the 7 attention variants on 3 synthetic cases x 2 split counts against FA3 (:775-789), then pin ATTN_FIXED (:819-821).
- SELFTEST is off (:211).
- Prepare and compile time on the judge are not published; their run passed (prepare <= 300 s, run <= 15 min).

Tuned tables:
- Python side: MOE_* (:119-141), BND_* (:144-146), ROUTER_*/COMBINE/PDL flags (:149-156), ATTN_* incl. ATTN_FIXED {slide 8, full 43} (:159-189), LT_TOKENS/POOL_SM_RESERVE/LT_PIN (:196-208).
- CUDA side: SLOT 43008 / NSLOT 4 / MOE_KPS32/64/128 = 4/2/2 / GROUP4 / NACC (:2059-2148), MOE_L2PF (:1683-1684), MOE_FINE_MASK (:1799), ROUTE_WARPS 16 (:3196), ATTN_ASTAGES / VOTEMAX / RR_HAND (:3667-3672, :4238).

Launch graph, about 14 launches per layer: qkv GEMM -> qk_prep -> attn_prep -> attn_fwd (+combine2 when split) -> o GEMM -> boundary (MODE 0, or MODE 1 at T>=8192) -> side stream [router Lt GEMM -> moe_route] beside the pooler GEMM -> gather+plan -> moe_fused (w13 and w2) -> Triton combine -> unpooler GEMM -> boundary (MODE 0, or MODE 2). PDL is on every custom kernel through okc_launch except boundary_rc and kv_gather (:1494-1520).

Running it as an okc check needs:
- line 1 as is;
- lines 2-3 kept, plus a modification-notice line;
- a nonce comment against dedup;
- the G0 or C1 compliance change;
- optionally a T>8 assert.
Nothing else: it is a single self-contained file.

H100 prescreen: our node has already run this file in-process beside ours (.worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-30-public-ladder/research/pf1-1030.md:3-5, :13-14, pfcmp.py). Caveat: its per-kernel sums are inflated by PDL overlap, so compare graph-replay wall times.

Critical files for this track:
- kernels/muse_prefill_layer.py (public)
- muse_prefill_layer/submission.py
- research/pf1-1030.md
- research/pf3-1030.md
- research/at7c-1030.md

### Public compliance flags

- HIGH (our ruling): the softmax denominator is the sum of E4M3-ROUNDED P, produced by a ones row in V^T inside the FP8 P.V wgmma (n=72): kernels/muse_prefill_layer.py:3625-3628, :3643, :3664, :3825, :3919, :4919. It is used on every own-attention route, i.e. all 8 scored rows (ATTN_FIXED :170 slide=8, full=43). This is exactly AT7b, which the user ruled out (sum unrounded FP32 P as FA3 softmax.h:181-184): .worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-30-public-ladder/research/at7b-1030.md:17-48, :75-79. The unchanged public file must not be sent even as a check; first apply G0 (FP32 unrounded-P denominator) or C1 (route attention to FA3 via _attention_norm_gate :648-654).
- LOW: late-max softmax on the exp-turn path (variant 43, full rows): P is computed against a stale running max, then a 0x7E saturation test re-does the tile exactly (kernels/muse_prefill_layer.py:3913-3917, VOTEMAX :3669). The softmax arithmetic stays exact, but P's E4M3 rounding grid differs from FA3's per-tile max. Our AT7f note flagged approximate maxima as a satfinite-clipping risk (research/at7fg-1030.md:27); their guard covers it. Document this for a reviewer.
- LOW: --use_fast_math covers the whole CUDA extension (kernels/muse_prefill_layer.py:1272-1273). That means ftz and approximate div/exp in the boundary, combine and routing sigmoid (expf :3218), plus SiLU via tanh.approx.f32 (:1723-1727). The errors are below BF16 resolution, the same class as our fsilu note (research/compliance-audit-1030.md:79). RoPE uses double sincos (:3440), so it is exact.
- LATENT (off-shape): there is no T<=8 path. kernel_fn (kernels/muse_prefill_layer.py:921-1023) always runs the BF16 hi/lo router on BF16 h and the full unpooler, while SGLang at T<=8 uses an FP32 router input and unpooler[:1024] (muse_prefill_layer/baseline.py:13-15, :133-134, :316-328; TASK.md 'T <= 8'). This is unreachable at scored/test T>=200, and our file asserts T>8 (muse_prefill_layer/submission.py:8342-8343). Add the same assert to any public-derived check: the same BF16-router-at-T<=8 issue withdrew 43 decode runs (public-ladder FINDINGS.md:36).
- PROCESS: our standing rules forbid sending their file or anything derived from it to okc check/submit (.worktrees/arch-mirror/campaigns/2026-10-31-arch-mirror/PLAN.md:113-115; campaigns/2026-10-30-rank-ladder/PLAN.md:93, :119). The user's request for a separate check-only track reads as lifting this, but it needs an explicit go. Keep the Apache-2.0 attribution header (kernels/muse_prefill_layer.py:1-3), add a modification notice (Apache-2.0 4b), and never let a public-derived file reach our board or master (our repo has no NOTICE file: arch-mirror PLAN.md:41).
- PROCESS: content dedup. An unchanged copy can return an earlier result (muse_prefill_layer/TASK.md:108; the result JSON carries content_hash/duplicate_of, muse_prefill_layer/hypotheses/BEST.result.json:7-8). Add a nonce comment to A/A controls.

### Candidates the verifier added

- C1 with our fa3c contiguous path on full rows. The agent prices C1 using OUR contiguous-FA3 times, but C1 as written routes to the public's paged page_size=1 FA3 (public :626-645). Wire the full classes to FA3's contiguous varlen call after a gather. The public already compiles an unused CUDA kv_gather_kernel (:3601, binding :1387); our Triton _kv_gather_contig is the alternative. Measured gain: -47/-235/-397 us at ftf0/32/64 and about -28 us at t4f, about +0.3% headline back on the compliance base. Evidence: done/neutral/2026-10-25T152235Z-fa3-full-contiguous-kv-triton-gather-gated.md:117, :139; profiles/submission.9f93484606cd4f9e.txt:48, :85 (gather 10.4/43.9 us). Compliance: our audit cleared fa3c (research/compliance-audit-1030.md:66-68).
- Per-class cheapest compliant base instead of all-FA3 (C1) or all-G0. After G0, set ATTN_FIXED=None so the public's own _tune_attn (:792-808) races the FA3 'ref' path against the G0 variants per class. full_short/full_batch are timed at T=753; full_long at 16384. Pin the winners afterwards: their comment at :820 says prepare races add noise. G0 costs about -0.3..-2.0% and C1 about -2.3..-2.6%, so a mix could cut the compliance tax. Caveat: slide is timed only at T=16384 (:796), so t1s needs G1's separate class split.
- Rows where our judge time beats theirs: none. All 8 prefill rows are slower on our side (ours/theirs 1.11-1.21, research/gap-ledger-20261030.txt:56-63). At the stage level there are only two candidates. (a) t1s sliding attention: this is G1, unproven. (b) Our SGLang routing_kernel at ft, 39.3/40.1 us (profiles :144, :163), against the public's route at 67 us (FACTS:136). Their moe_route also writes local ids, positions and counts, which our align phases do separately, and at ft it runs on the side stream beside the pooler. So (b) is <=0.1% headline: too small.
- Considered and rejected, so not a candidate. (1) Our S2 2-CTA/SM low-class tile (CLAIMS.md:79, flat on the judge): the public already built a 2-CTA/SM feasibility version of the fused kernel and left it off (SKINNY_TEST=False, :136, :393-394). (2) Our 16-row bucket granularity against their 25% padding at T=753 (FACTS:138): their per-token MMA32 path is off (MOE_MMA32 0, :2164), and N32 items keep a 9.2 us floor with or without decode or MMA (FACTS:139), so padding is not the cost.

### Verifier corrections to the unmet-gains list

- Fused expert kernel, 'inferred split': the claim that removing the w13 tail recovers 6-13% (~190-410 us at fts) overstates it. LESSONS.md:195 records span max/mean 1.06-1.13, but the ingest cap is partly a shared L2 limit, so CTA-balance ideas give a few percent at most. pf1-1030.md:145-146 puts the fused launch at 15-30 us at t1s.
- Fused expert kernel: 'X-stationary order <= ~5% of w13 (3790.9 vs 3614.2, pf3-1030.md:39,43)' is mis-described. That difference is the routing-skew penalty (ring rate 8.7 -> 6.3 TB/s, pf3-1030.md:40-46), not a measurement of item order. X-stationary order also leaves L2->SM ingest per FLOP unchanged, because each CTA still ingests its own copy of the X tile.
- Fused expert kernel, mechanism text: the tile list omits 96 and 224 (wgmma_rs<96> :1769, wgmma_rs<224> :1790, tile-width comment :2066-2069). The L2-prefetch note (+0.29%) is at :1690, not :1683; the inventory's ':1683-1688' should be :1688-1694. The +0.29% is a board-level search figure, below one run's noise.
- Own attention, full rows: pf1-1030.md:38-40 compares the public attn_fwd kernel alone (355/1363 us) against our FA3. The public's attn_prep staging (:3704-3850, a full swizzled K/V re-layout of up to ~164 MB per pass at t4f) and combine2 are not stated as included, while our gather (10.4/43.9 us) is separate. The stage gap may therefore be smaller than 56/235 us. The compliant share must also subtract G0's cost (+2..+12% of attention). The 'C1 cross-check ~1.9%' is circular: it reuses the same inferred numbers, and it corrects to about 2.3-2.6%.
- Own attention, cross-check that supports the ft-full assumption: from row differences, public ftf64 - ftf0 = 5768 us against ours 6628 us (RESULTS.md prefill table; gap ledger :57-63). That puts the public's attention increment about 13% below ours, consistent with -14..-17%.
- Glue diet: at T=16384 the public's boundaries stream 3.4-4.1 TB/s (FACTS:137). Ours run at about 4.0 TB/s (1.61 GB per boundary in ~405 us, profiles :118-119). So the boundary part of the glue gap is byte removal (the recompute item), not kernel speed. campaign/arch-mirror CLAIMS.md:88's '0.70-0.83x of HBM rate' divides by the 4.8 TB/s spec peak. At t1s the cited public boundaries (46 us, H100) against our judge 44 us (H200) is not a same-hardware gap.
- Glue diet, fts: the share uses 50 us. The profile shows a 41 us torch elementwise kernel the public lacks (profiles :125, :143, :162), plus gaps of qk 43.6 vs 28, boundaries +36 and gather/combine +5 each. It also shows our routing_kernel (~40 us) faster than their route (67 us). Net fts glue gap is about 70-100 us. Still small: fts is 0.0025%/us.
- Router chain at turn rows: the router GEMM is at parity (our judge 15.1 us at t1s, profile 0, vs their H100 17 us, pf1-1030.md:27). The rest of our chain on the node was shimmed (pf1-1030.md:17-18), so the ~10 us t1s gap is unsupported. The public's own comment (:197-200) says their chain also lands on the critical path at small T.
- Judge-noise row: the listed shares (43 + 15 + 17-21 + 3-5 + 2.5 + 1 + 7) leave 5.5-11.5% unexplained, not ~12%. The router item overlaps the glue item, as the agent notes, so 'unexplained' is a floor, not a measurement.
- General: the per-us headline rates implied by RESULTS.md weights are t1s 0.037, t1f 0.0128, t4s 0.0116, t4f 0.0040, fts 0.0025 and each ft-full row 0.0003 %/us. Every graft except C1 and G0 lands below 0.4% headline at these rates. That is under the ~1% the public found necessary to move a row (NEG:186-190).

### Why the gap persists here (inventory agent)

1) The gap is a stack of architectural differences, not one missing mechanism.
- Size: ln(1.8167/1.5721) = 0.1446. Per row (gap ledger, research/gap-ledger-20261030.txt:55-64): t1s x1.043, fts x1.034, t4s x1.022, t1f x1.018, t4f x1.015, ft-full rows x1.003-1.006.
- No row of ours beats theirs. All 8 rows are x1.11-1.21 slower: t1s 709.6 vs 585.1, fts 10288.8 vs 8879.6 us. So the strongest graft class (a row where our mechanism wins) is empty. The only per-kernel win is FA3 sliding attention at T=753 (graft G1).
- Inferred decomposition from this read:
  - fused expert kernel ~43% (x1.064)
  - glue + launch diet + PDL + router chain ~17-21%
  - own full-row attention ~15% (<= ~2% in a compliant FA3 form: forcing their file onto FA3 costs it only ~1.9%)
  - T=16384 cuBLASLt pins ~2.5%
  - boundary recompute ~1%
  - their fast draw ~7% (1.8167 vs same-file re-checks 1.7958/1.8002, FACTS:62)
  - ~12% unexplained (it includes our slow t1s draw: 709.6 scored vs 661.9 in a same-code check)
- The realistic target is ~1.80/1.572 = x1.145.

2) Every rung on our base was worth less than one run's noise.
- PF1 +0.3% (pf1-1030.md:122-123).
- PF2 flat on the judge (CLAIMS.md:83).
- PF3 +0.35-0.45% (pf3-build-1030.md:113-118).
- S2 ~+1%, flat twice on the judge (CLAIMS.md:79-80).
- FA3 replacement EV < 0.6% (arch-mirror CLAIMS.md:85).
- Glue items 0.2-1% each (CLAIMS.md:60, :64, :65; branch :88).
- The bar: one judge run's margin is ~1.24% (pf1:123), and rows drift 1.5-2.5% (CLAIMS.md:23). Even alygg77 could not land +0.31..+0.41% local gains on this same file (NEGATIVE_RESULTS.md:186-190).
- So x1.145 at <= 1.2% per landing needs about 12 landings. The rank-ladder put the all-problem odds near 5% (rank-ladder PLAN.md:84-90).

3) The mechanisms we refuted are mostly the public's own pieces, measured outside their structure.

| mechanism | our result | where the public uses it |
|---|---|---|
| PDL | refuted, CLAIMS.md:47/:59 | every kernel, :1430-1438 |
| atomic queue + LPT order | refuted, :49/:61/:62 | :2764, :129 |
| register ring + L2 weight prefetch | refuted, :68 | :2258-2312, :2789-2793 |
| wait<1> (RS2) | refuted, :80 | :2302-2313 |
| bulk-store epilogue | refuted, :53/:66 | publisher warp, :2808-2836 |
| N=256 token tiles | flat on judge, :83 | :2070 |

- Inside one fused launch these interact. There is no w13->w2 barrier, a single tail is shared by both GEMMs, and GEMM2 items are gated per tile (:2774-2781).
- On our two-launch, statically scheduled rega, each piece alone stayed inside noise.
- This is the same lesson arch-mirror recorded (PLAN.md:30-33): transplanted values failed 2/2, single decisions failed, and only side-by-side measurement found the gaps.

4) One structural difference was never tested: item order. Inferred, not measured.
- Ours: rega walks an expert's chunks fastest, i.e. weight-stationary (muse_prefill_layer/submission.py:2723-2741).
- Theirs: n-tiles fastest over biggest-first token tiles, i.e. X-stationary (:2764-2771, :2956-3034).
- Our rotation test kept chunk-minor neighbours (done/neutral/2026-10-26T223951Z-experts-rega-round-rotated-schedule.md:24).
- Why it could matter: on the judge's skewed ft routing the hot expert has ~8.6k of 36.6k local routes (pf3-1030.md:32). That is ~67 chunks x 1 MB of X, which can exceed a ~50 MB L2. This fits the ring-rate drop from 8.7 to 6.3 TB/s (pf3-1030.md:48).
- Bound: the effect is capped near the real-vs-balanced gap, w13 3790.9 vs 3614.2 us (~5%, pf3-1030.md:39, :43). It does not explain the whole expert gap. A fused launch removing the 6-13% w13 tail (CLAIMS.md:49) is the other large inferred part.

5) Transfer failures ate the rungs that were built.
- PF2's in-prepare race used balanced routing and read x0.93; the judge's routing is skewed and PF2 went flat (CLAIMS.md:83).
- PF1's random-input GEMM isolation over-predicted by 2.5x (pf1:101-120).
- Cold-L2 synthetic gates passed but were flat on the judge (CLAIMS.md:50).
- Judge rega runs at ~0.82-0.89 of node time (pf2:90).
- The node cannot reproduce judge prepare: rega switches off without --nobudget (pf3-build:107-110).
- Our H100 view of their stages is kernel sums, and PDL inflates those.

6) There is no oracle.
- Prefill was cut from arch-mirror for lack of one (PLAN.md:125) and parked in union-confirm (LOG:25, :32).
- The only same-input comparison is pf1's stage table (pf1-1030.md:22-29). Their expert kernel was never decomposed against ours.

7) Compliance and budget.
- Their attention's ones-row denominator (:3625-3628) is outside our ruling (at7b:75-79), so their full-row attention can only be mirrored in the AT7c form.
- Bytes are not binding.

Steer for the post-reset check-only track:
- (a) Never send the raw file. Base the track on C1 (FA3 attention, ~1.765 expected) or G0. Pair every arm with a same-hour nonce A/A (FACTS:62, :63-64).
- (b) Grafts ranked by expected value:

| graft | expected headline |
|---|---|
| G1, FA3 sliding at T<=4096 | +0.3..+0.5% |
| G5, PF3 w2 slab share at ft | +0.2..+0.56%, speculative |
| G8, EXPMODE 8 | -0.3..+0.66% |
| G6 order / G3 / G4 / G7 | <= +0.2% each, prior ~0 |

- (c) Mimic arms on our own base, ordered by share:
  1. X-stationary item order in rega decode_item (bit-exact, a few lines).
  2. Fused w13+w2 persistent launch with per-tile done counters and an atomic LPT queue.
  3. The qk_prep / route / gather+plan / PDL launch-diet bundle.
  4. A compliant own full-row attention.

### Verifier: inventory misclassifications

- pdl-chain-expert-block: 'public_positive_contradicts_our_refutation' should be 'public_has_it, isolated value unpublished'. PDL is on every launch (:1430-1438), but the docs contain no PDL A/B. Their only PDL-adjacent knob is +0.06..0.09% (ATTN_EARLY_Q :3693). NEG:27 says side-stream joins into PDL chains cost >=2 us. Our flat judge read (CLAIMS.md:47) is not contradicted.
- rega-schedule-rotation-lpt-atomic: 'contradicts' is overstated. The fused w13->w2 kernel needs a dynamic queue anyway (work counter + per-tile done flags, zb at :872-873; counter :2764). MOE_ORDER=1 is a 'measured default' (:112, :129) with no published magnitude. Our refutation concerns per-CTA imbalance on a two-launch kernel (CLAIMS.md:49/:61/:62; LESSONS.md:195). Reclassify as 'public_has_it in a different architecture, value unpublished'.
- rega-RS2-wait1: not a contradiction. Our refutation was a register-budget build block, not a measured loss: the judge's ptxas spilled 80/36 B at the 168-reg cap (CLAIMS.md:80). The public's consumers run at setmaxnreg 232 (:2868), so that blocker never arises. Reclassify as 'public_has_it; consistent with our reopen condition'.
- rega-register-ring-l2-prefetch: 'contradicts' is partly right but overstated. GROUP4 (:2125-2128) is the same 'convert stage s+1 under stage s's group' idea. It adds independent NACC2/NACC4 accumulators (:2131-2148), which our RR lacked. The only published value is L2PF +0.29% (:1690), below one run's noise. Reclassify as 'public_has_a_variant (adds independent accumulators); value mostly unpublished'.
- rega-w2-early-gs-loads: 'public_lacks' is misleading. Most of what arm a fixed was the per-chunk sorted_ids __ldg in the scatter loop (done/neutral/2026-10-27T133154Z-experts-rega-w2-epilogue-prefetch-bulk-store.md:12-19). The public's GEMM2 epilogue has no sid gather: it writes rows contiguously (:2415-2427), and its gscale load is covered by the barrier that follows (:2340-2342). Reclassify as 'public_lacks_the_problem / not_applicable'.
- own-attention-vs-fa3-full: 'contradicts' holds only at kernel level, and it is compliance-confounded. Part of the public's speed is the ones-row E4M3 denominator our ruling forbids (:3625-3628; research/at7b-1030.md:75-79). pf1-1030.md:38 times attn_fwd only, with staging (:3704-3850) not stated as included. A compliant variant's edge has never been measured.
- ladder-pf3-slab-share-ft: 'public_lacks' is literally true, but the public uses the dual form of reuse: wide token tiles up to N=256 (kps_for :2070, wgmma_rs<256> :1762). With 2 consumer WGs at acc[128] (:2150), the register file cannot hold both 256 weight rows and 256 tokens. It should read 'public_uses_the_token-side_dual; not combinable'.
